## Open

_Each entry carries an effort tag: **XS** ≤15 min, one edit and no design call · **S** ~30-60 min, a small change plus its test · **M** ~2-4 h, several files or a design call inside a known shape · **L** ~a session, a design decision, a migration or a new surface · **XL** wants its own plan. `· after X` means the size is what remains once X lands, not today's cost. Tags are estimates for an agent session including tests and doc sync, sized 2026-08-14 against the entry text, with the XS ones checked against the working tree._

- [ ] 2026-09-14 [compose-passes-almost-no-config] `M ~2h · decision first` **`docker-compose.yml` declares no `env_file:`, so most of `app/config.py` can never be configured on a deployed stack.** Compose reads `.env` only to interpolate `${VAR}` into the compose file; a key reaches a container only if a service's `environment:` block names it. Verified 2026-09-14 by grepping the whole file: `BC_BASE_URL`, `BC_USERNAME`, `BC_PASSWORD`, `BC_WRITE_ENABLED`, `UPLOAD_MAX_MB`, `GATE_THRESHOLD_HIGH`/`MED`, `MODELS_*`, `RESOLVE_NAME_*`, `DISCOVER_*` (except `DISCOVER_HOLD`), `FETCH_*`, `BUDGET_*`, `QUEUE_*`, `STORAGE_ADAPTER` and `SOURCE_ADAPTER` appear **zero times**. Consequences today: `bc.push-drift` cannot be enabled by any `.env` value, the documented BC OData ingest route cannot reach an endpoint without `-e` on the command, and `.env.example` reads as a configuration surface that mostly is not one. `tests/test_compose_config.py` pins only that every `SCHEDULER_*` key reaches `worker` and `web` -- the same bug class, fixed once, narrowly. **Decision before work:** add `env_file: .env` to the three app services (one line, but it also hands every container every secret in the file), or extend the explicit `environment:` blocks key by key (verbose, keeps the blast radius visible, matches the existing style). Then widen the compose test to the whole config surface, not just `SCHEDULER_*`. `docs/dev/deployment.md` § 3 documents the current behaviour as a warning until this lands.

(2) `ALERTS_WEBHOOK_URL` is wired into both `worker` and `web` (`docker-compose.yml:150,292`) and is the only mechanism that announces a dead-lettered job to anyone, yet it appears nowhere in `.env.example` -- so nobody setting up a server is prompted to set it.
- [ ] 2026-09-14 [rehearsal-proves-less-than-it-claims] `S ~1h · script + CLAUDE.md line` **Two of `scripts/bringup-rehearsal.sh`'s assertions cannot fail, and it never exercises the crons.** Step 8 asserts `count(*) FROM item_group WHERE canonical_manufacturer IS NOT NULL` > 0, but the column is `text NOT NULL` (migration 002) and RESOLVE's `_alias_lookup` self-seeds a miss by inserting the raw BC code as its own canonical name (`app/handlers/resolve.py`), so a run with `playbooks sync` skipped -- the exact trap the rehearsal exists to catch -- passes that check with every group named `011`. Replace it with a join against `vendor_master.code`, as `docs/dev/deployment.md` § 9 now does. Second: the drain loop calls `runner.run_once` directly, never `runner.main()`, so `arm_crons()` never runs and the rehearsal never sees the cron set; relatedly, **`coverage-scan` has no migration seed at all** (055 seeds 7, 064 and 065 add 2) and exists only because `arm_crons` creates it, which makes CLAUDE.md's "migrations 054/055, then 064 and 065" overstate what migrations establish.

Not theoretical against a client-supplied export: the 38 playbooks now claim **46 distinct BC codes**, while migration 049's comment records the last check as 39 codes on 2026-08-26. Cheap fix: diff the claimed codes against the loaded master and raise the same readable error naming the missing ones.

In `docs/samples/bc-api-2026-09-02-allitems.json` that property is **present and empty on all three records**, while `manufacturerCode` carries `"011"` — which is what `docs/2026-09-03-bc-api-integration.md` §3.1 concluded from the same payload ("`pteManufCodePrimary` is `Šifra proizvajalca (primarni)`: 16 of 19.091 rows, two values `077-A`/`077-B`"). `_assert_profile_matches` checks presence, not emptiness, so the wrong choice mirrors the whole catalogue with `manufacturer_raw` null: no group is named, no playbook binds, the REF gate can never establish a manufacturer, and INGEST reports success. Either b-s.si populated the field after 09-02 or the answer was wrong, and nothing in the repo can tell which. **Do:** `curl` the endpoint with `?$top=1` once it is reachable, save it under `docs/samples/`, run `python -m tools bc-api`, then either keep the field or revert to `manufacturerCode`. Consider making the guard reject a profile key that is empty on the first record, which would have caught this. See `docs/dev/deployment.md` § 6.4.

- [ ] 2026-09-11 [supersession-ignores-coverage-subject] `L · RULED 2026-09-14, spec first` **RULING (Denis, 2026-09-14): per-item supersession.** A newer declaration replaces an older one **only for the articles it names**, only when genuinely newer, and only within the same type and regulation; the document turns `superseded` only when no production link of its own survives. His reasoning, which settles the cheaper alternative: *"we can't request a manufacturer to always cover all the same devices — what if some go out of production?"* Requiring the newer document to name every article of the older one would treat ordinary product lifecycle as an anomaly. Measured the same day over every pair of production documents sharing a group, type and regulation — 41 pairs: **11 identical article sets, 1 superset, 1 partial overlap, 28 with no shared article**, so the group is the wrong subject 68% of the time. Work: `item_document` gains a link-level `superseded` status and `superseded_by`; C6 amended from "coverage subject" to the article set; **every reader of `document.status='superseded'` must learn the link-level state** (the compliance card, `/documents`, the item page, `/staging`'s unpublished-links panel, `item_document_production`, `bc_fields`) or a screen shows a document as current for articles it no longer covers. Spec before code. Original entry follows. **A declaration naming fewer articles supersedes a wider one outright, and 94 items lost their current DoC.** 2026-08-21: GATE superseded doc 162 (IVOCLAR, cert `G15 043306 0282`, 78 production links over 23 groups) with doc 815 (`ivoclar-doc-2026.pdf`, 1 item, 1 group). 2026-08-19: doc 415 (KOMET, 18 links over 10 groups) by doc 377 (15 links). `_current_production_doc` (`app/handlers/validate.py:675-694`) takes any production document sharing ONE group with the candidate as "the current one"; `validate.py:1043-1044` sets `supersedes`; `_apply_supersession` (`app/handlers/gate.py:679-694`) re-checks only (type, regulation) and supersedes the whole document. Invariant 5 and AC8 require an identical coverage subject. The 96 old links stay `production` but `item_document_production` drops them, so the items fall back to manufacturer scope silently (this is why the 09-04 state's 1.900 DoC figure is 1.806 through the view). Ruling needed: supersede per item link (only the links the successor also covers), or refuse when the successor's subject is narrower and route to manual. Then repair 162/415, which are append-only by invariant 4, via the chosen mechanism, and add a test where the successor covers fewer groups. **Re-read later on 2026-09-11, two corrections.** (1) Doc 815 is not a supplier document: it came from `tools/seed_fake_mailbox.py` (job 35279, source `email:<seed-reply@ivoclar.example>`), so 77 of the 94 items were lost to a dev test email; docs 832 and 839 are seeded copies of the same file. (2) All four supersessions in the DB are wrong, not two. KOMET 375 (533047), 379 (533049), 415 (533079) and 377 (533048) are four different RA 810 declarations, and the chain 375→415, 379→415, 415→377 joins documents that share a catalogue group, never an article (415 ∩ 377 = 0 items). PRD C6 itself says "overlapping coverage subject" (`docs/dentalia-pipeline-contract-prd-v3.md:431`), which is what the code implements and what contradicts invariant 5's "identical". **Recommended, awaiting Denis:** per-link supersession. `item_document` gains a `superseded` status and `superseded_by`; a document becomes `superseded` only when no production link remains; C6 amended. Repair: one audited correction returning 162, 375, 379, 415 and 377 to production and 815, 832, 839 to `rejected`.

- [ ] 2026-09-04 [crawl-index-fetch-not-ledgered] `S ~1h · contract + a measurement` **A crawl recipe's index page is fetched once per requesting group and never enters the fetch ledger.** Found by the 2026-09-04 flow-map pass (§5, undocumented). `discover._crawl_playbook` calls `fetcher.get(url)` directly and writes no `fetch_log` row, so invariant 6's "unchanged content never re-fetched" does not reach it: EDENTA has **103** groups and NSK **136**, and each `discover.group` for those manufacturers re-requests the same library page. Migration 061 now caches the expensive half — the T1 link judgement, keyed on a hash of the harvest — so what remains is the HTTP request and the manufacturer's goodwill, not tokens. The politeness lease bounds the RATE, never the total. Note that the probe (`app/handlers/playbook_probe.py`) states the OPPOSITE rule for itself and explains why ("A probe must leave no trace in the ledger and nothing in the archive"), so the two consumers of `app.crawl` behave differently by design and neither behaviour is written in a contract. Decide which rule the crawl follows and say so in PRD §3; if it should be ledgered, `url_normalized` would be the index URL and the recency window would then suppress the repeat fetch for free.

- [ ] 2026-09-04 [discover-should-rank-doc-ec-iso-above-ifu] `M ~half day · Denis 2026-09-04` **DISCOVER retrieves whatever the manufacturer publishes openly, and that is overwhelmingly the IFU.** The 09-03 slice: 83 documents, **49 IFU against 26 DoC**, 4 EC, 2 ISO. Ruling: *"keep ifus, not primary, but keep — the goal in searches should be to FIND DOC, EC, ISO. but keep ifu if found. we need to force it to search for docs more."* So this is a RANKING and QUERY change, never a filter — an IFU that turns up is still archived and linked. Two halves: (1) `_query_for` (`app/handlers/discover.py`) should bias the query toward declaration/certificate language rather than the bare product name; (2) `_default_rank`, the live T1 ranker, should score a candidate that looks like a DoC/EC/ISO above one that looks like an IFU. Measure the before/after on the same group sample, because the only honest test of this is the hit mix, not the hit rate. **HALF DONE 2026-09-07.** Half (2), the ranking, shipped for the CRAWL rung and is measured: `[crawl-ai-link-triage]` above. Half (1), the QUERY, is untouched — `_query_for` still asks one question per group ending `declaration of conformity pdf`. Open question before building it: per-type queries would roughly double the search spend for an unmeasured gain, and the crawl result suggests the cheaper win is authoring recipes so libraries are read directly rather than searched at all. **DEFERRED by Denis 2026-09-07.** Not building the query half. Revisit only if the two authored crawl recipes fail to shift the mix — reading a library directly is the cheaper path to the same documents, and it is now measured (NSK: 129 declarations in a 200-link budget, against 118 declarations and 60 junk links in page order).
- [ ] 2026-09-04 [one-basic-credential-for-every-reviewer] `S ~1h · DEFERRED by Denis 2026-09-04 ("skip for now")` **Caddy carries one `basic_auth` pair, so `audit_log.decided_by` records `admin` for every officer.** All 22 human audit rows are `admin`; the other 2.134 were written by `gate` (673) or by hand-run repair tools (1.461). `basic_auth` supports multiple named users and `web/app.py:1938-1949` already reads `X-Forwarded-User`, so this is configuration, not architecture. - [ ] 2026-09-04 [gc-declarations-lose-their-issue-date] `M ~3h · measured, cause identified` **55 of the 58 production documents with no `validity_from` are GC EUROPE, 53 of them Declarations of Conformity.** Not scattered extraction noise, one manufacturer's template. **32 of the 55 DO carry a date in their text** — `12/02/2026` under a multilingual `Datum; Date; Date; Data; Datums; Data; Datum; Tarihler` label — and T0 misses it twice over: the label sits on the line BELOW the value separated by dot leaders, and `datum` is absent from `_FROM_LABELS` (`app/extract/t0_templates.py:180`) while appearing in `_FOOTER_WORDS` (`:976`) as a word to IGNORE. The place-and-date rule (`:268`) cannot help either: it requires `City, DD/MM/YYYY` ending the line, and GC prints the place and the date in different blocks. Per ground truth §7.1 the Annex IV point-10 date IS the date of issue and belongs in `validity_from`, so these are genuine misses. Careful: this is the exact field whose neighbour caused the 2026-08-12 incident — the fix must put the date in `validity_from`, never `validity_to`. The remaining 23 have no date in the text layer at all and are a separate question. **Re-checked 2026-09-11: still live, not residue.** All 53 were extracted on 2026-08-17; the per-playbook `date_labels` mechanism arrived 08-19 and `playbooks/gc.json` has none, and no later commit touches GC's date path. A label alone would not fix it anyway, because the value sits above the label.

- [ ] 2026-09-04 [no-alert-path-off-this-machine] `M ~2h · go-live blocker for anything unattended` **If the worker dies, nobody finds out until a human opens the UI.** Zero hits for Slack, webhook, PagerDuty, ntfy, telegram or any SMTP client across `app/` and `web/`. The signals that exist are a red chip inside a password-protected page and a stale `service_heartbeat` row — both pull, not push. `service_heartbeat` also retains rows from dead containers (five rows live, four of them from containers that no longer exist), so "a worker heartbeat exists" is not the question "a worker is alive"; anything reading it must check freshness. - [ ] 2026-09-02 [eudamed-ref-normalisation] `M ~3h · analysed and deliberately deferred` **Normalising the EUDAMED reference join would match 108 more KOMET articles, and produce zero new requests.** The join is raw `COALESCE(mfr_ref, item_ref) = eudamed_mirror.reference`, which matches nothing for KOMET: we hold `204 F04L25 025`, EUDAMED publishes `F04L25.204.025.R0`. Two steps bridge it -- `_fold_bur_code` (already live in `app/handlers/validate.py`, folds shank/figure/size to dotted) and stripping EUDAMED's trailing variant suffix (`.R0`, `.K2`). Measured: 0 -> 108 of 258, behind 10 Basic UDI-DIs, with **zero base refs spanning more than one Basic UDI-DI** and corroborated independently by KOMET's own 3.800-row `1-Where to find DoC` crosswalk. Applying the fold universally is safe -- it returns non-bur codes unchanged, and CARL MARTIN (2.389) and IVOCLAR (899) were measured identical with and without. **Deferred because all 108 already hold production DoCs** (measured), so it buys audit evidence -- unknown becomes provably covered -- and no work to chase. The remaining 150 KOMET articles are shank-`000` catalogue entries that are not bur codes at all; no normalisation reaches them, and `_fold_bur_code`'s own docstring says it must not invent a shape for them. Design if picked up: a `GENERATED ALWAYS AS (regexp_replace(reference,'\.[A-Z][0-9]+$',''))` column on `eudamed_mirror` plus an IMMUTABLE SQL `fold_bur_code`, no new job and no backfill -- but that puts the fold in SQL *and* Python, so it needs a test asserting the two agree over the real refs, and a migration recreating `eudamed_article_status` and its three dependents in order. Only KOMET carries the suffix today (1.581 refs; 0 for the other three), so this generalises when a second manufacturer needs it, not before. Denis, 2026-09-02: do the certificate register first.

- [ ] 2026-09-02 [shared-test-container-kills-in-flight-suites] `S ~1h · hit live, twice-costly` **Any session bringing the stack up silently kills every other session's running suite, and the victim sees a 0-byte file rather than an error.** `scripts/test.sh` `exec`s into the single long-lived `test` container and never `up`s it -- the script is innocent and says so in its header -- but `docker compose --profile test up -d test` is the line the script PRINTS when the stack is down, so any agent following that instruction recreates the container and terminates the exec'd pytest of every other checkout. Observed 2026-09-02: `dentalia-test-1` recreated at 12:13:22Z while this session's full suite was running; the container was left holding `sleep infinity` with no pytest process, `docker logs` empty, output file 0 bytes. The failure is indistinguishable from a slow run, which is what makes it expensive -- it was read as semaphore contention and waited on for ~26 minutes before the process table showed there was nothing to wait for. The flock semaphore at `/tmp/pytest-slots` deliberately spans worktrees, so cross-session sharing is by design; this hazard is the same sharing without a guard. Cheapest fixes to weigh: have `test.sh` refuse to start when a pytest process is already live in the container, have it print a bring-up line that is idempotent rather than recreating (`docker compose --profile test start test`), or give it a non-zero exit and a loud message when its own exec dies without pytest having written a summary line. Reported independently by two sessions the same day.
- [ ] 2026-09-02 [eudamed-sweep-is-one-long-transaction] **CORRECTED AGAIN 2026-09-09 — the timeout mitigation IS in effect now; the 09-04 correction below was true when written and stopped being true the same day.** A 2026-09-04 change threaded `cfg.queue` into the runner, so `app/workers/runner.py:70` passes `visibility_timeout_s=queue_cfg.visibility_timeout_s` and the live worker reclaims at 1800s (`[queue-timeout-fix-never-reached-the-worker]`, closed 2026-09-09). **Both mitigations are therefore real again, and the structural fix is still not built** — `handle_eudamed_sweep` still walks every SRN and page in one call with no `_deferred` return, and `eudamed_sweep_state` still has no `next_page` column. Superseded text follows. ~~CORRECTED 2026-09-04 — ONE mitigation is real, not two. `visibility_timeout_s = 1800` was never in effect.~~ `app/config.py:421` declares 1800, but `app/workers/runner.py:57` calls `queue.claim(conn, worker_id)` and passes nothing, so the live worker uses `app/queue.py:116`'s own default of **300** — the same number that reclaimed job 35679 at 301s. The `last_swept_at` guard IS real and in place. See `[queue-timeout-fix-never-reached-the-worker]`. Was: RE-CHECKED 2026-09-02 — the two mitigations are real and still in place. **The structural fix is not built**: `handle_eudamed_sweep` still walks every SRN and page in one call with no `_deferred` return, and `eudamed_sweep_state` has no `next_page` column.** `M ~3h · the structural fix behind today's mitigation` **A sweep fetches every page inside ONE transaction and one claim, so a reclaim discards all of it.** Measured live: job 35679 (IVOCLAR, 11.366 devices / 38 pages) was reclaimed at exactly 301s against a 300s `visibility_timeout_s`, and because `app/workers/runner.py` wraps the handler in one transaction every page already stored rolled back -- the mirror's 544 IVOCLAR rows still carried `synced_at` from 2026-08-25 afterwards. Two things were done the same day and NEITHER is the fix: `visibility_timeout_s` raised 300 -> 1800 (`app/config.py`, global, so a genuinely dead worker now holds any job 30 min instead of 5), and an empty sweep no longer stamps `last_swept_at` (`app/handlers/eudamed.py`, the guard that would have made this visible instead of silent). The real fix is per-page: store a page, commit, return `{"_deferred": True}` and come back for the next one, so no single claim is ever long and a restart costs one page instead of 38. Progress cannot live in the payload (invariant 9, immutable after enqueue) -- it belongs in `eudamed_sweep_state` as a `next_page` column, the same side-table pattern `batch_ref` uses for Batch API ids. `scheduler.tick` is the working precedent for the self-defer half. Until then a sweep bigger than ~1800s of paging still fails the same way, and CARL MARTIN (3.594 devices) is the next one to try.
- [ ] 2026-09-02 [eudamed-sweep-empty-page-cause-unknown] **RE-CHECKED 2026-09-02 — the guard shipped: `_is_last`/`_contradicts_empty` (`app/handlers/eudamed.py:166-223`) raises when the body is empty but the envelope claims more, and `_pause_between_pages` (:141-163) now throttles paging. **What remains is the item's own tail** — what triggers the 307 — which needs one live re-test after an idle period.** `S ~1h · needs one observed run` **Why IVOCLAR's page 0 came back empty is not established.** The guard shipped today stops an empty sweep reporting success, but it does not explain the emptiness. Re-running the exact request by hand minutes later returned a healthy page -- `totalElements 11366, totalPages 38, size 300, numberOfElements 300, last false` -- so the API was fine. Candidates not distinguished: a transient 200-with-empty-content from EUDAMED; the reclaim interleaving (attempt 2 claimed at 10:35:24.641 and finished 3ms later, while an httpx 200 for that same URL is logged at 10:35:35, eleven seconds AFTER the job was marked done); or a response the handler read as empty for another reason. The worker container was recreated around 11:00 and took most of its log with it, so the attempt-1/attempt-2 interleaving could not be reconstructed. Needs one sweep run under observation with the log retained -- CARL MARTIN is the cheap case at 3.594 devices. Note the guard now leaves such a sweep due rather than stamped, so a repeat is visible on the due list instead of silent. **CAUSE ESTABLISHED 2026-09-02, and it is not the reclaim: EUDAMED answers 200 with a well-formed but EMPTY `content` array.** Reproduced live in an interleaved host/container probe of the sweep's own URL (`srn=LI-MF-000000522`): round 1 returned 200 with a real payload from both positions, round 2 returned 200 with `"content" : [ ]` from the worker container, and rounds 3-6 returned HTTP 307 with an HTML "Network Error" page from BOTH positions at once. `_is_last()` reads an empty content array as a clean end of pagination, so the handler breaks out of the loop on page 0, stores nothing, and reports success -- exactly what jobs 35679 and 35701 did. **This supersedes two earlier readings, both wrong.** (a) It is not a container-egress block: the worker container fetched a real payload in rounds 1-2 (hostname verified against `docker ps -q`), and the worker's own log shows `httpx ... 200 OK` for the sweep URL, so the pipeline can reach EUDAMED. The 307 hits host and container in the same second, which puts it on the shared egress path, not Docker's. (b) The `httpx` 200 logged 11s after job 35679 and 10s after 35701 were 'done' is NOT an anomaly and needs no explanation: `finished_at` is `now()`, which in Postgres is transaction START time, and the runner wraps the whole handler in one transaction -- so a sweep that fetches for 10s always records a `finished_at` predating its own requests. **The fix is now specific**, and it is not the `no content key` case originally filed: treat a 200 whose `content` is empty while `totalElements > 0` (or while `number < totalPages - 1`) as an ERROR, not as the last page. The envelope carries `totalElements`, `totalPages`, `numberOfElements` and `last` -- page 0 of a real IVOCLAR sweep is `totalElements 11366, totalPages 38, numberOfElements 300, last false`, so a contradicting envelope is detectable without guessing. A 200 with no `content` key at all should be an error too. **Still open:** what triggers the 307. The pattern -- two clean rounds then a persistent block -- looks rate-induced and several sessions probed the host hard today, so `fetch.politeness_ms` for this host may be the real control. Needs one re-test after an idle period, not more probing.

Hit live: dentalia-64 tried to release CARL MARTIN, IVOCLAR and KOMET, got 403 "staff access required" from inside the web container, and correctly refused to forge `X-Forwarded-User` -- that header is the auth boundary and the 2026-08-25 change exists because it leaked once. **Distinct from `[eudamed-probe-not-operator-reachable]`**, which is a 400 for a manufacturer holding no trusted SRN; these three hold one and are refused on authentication alone. Cost of the gap: those three manufacturers cover 3.894 device items and EUDAMED currently touches ~197 of 4.265, so this single button is the largest coverage move on the board. Options: a `dentalia eudamed-sweep <manufacturer>` CLI that stamps `released_at` with the operator's name (keeps RULING 42's "a human releases every run", moves it off the browser), or accept that release stays a UI-only action and Denis presses the buttons. Do NOT resolve it by letting a second session set the auth header.

- [ ] 2026-08-31 [mfr-scope-link-loop-is-per-row] `M ~2h · INSERT ... SELECT, removes the loop` **`gate._derive_mfr_scope_links` writes one round-trip per item in a Python `for` loop**, inside the runner's transaction. Measured floor on this machine 2026-08-31 (trivial `SELECT 1`, so a real upsert costs more): **0,503 ms per round-trip → ≥1,29 s for doc 816's 2.567 CARL MARTIN items today, ≥7,58 s for the same manufacturer at ~100k items.** Tens of seconds holding a write transaction open is the failure mode. Pre-existing and untouched by 053 — that work neither causes nor worsens it, and was deliberately kept out of scope. Fix is a single `INSERT ... SELECT ... ON CONFLICT`, which deletes the loop entirely; the sticky-status CASE logic in `_upsert_link` has to move into the statement rather than being lost. - [ ] 2026-08-27 [eudamed-register-churns-the-playbook-cache] **RE-CHECKED 2026-09-02 — unchanged; the line drifted. The per-actor `_MANUFACTURER_UPSERT` is now `app/handlers/eudamed.py:509` (was :432), still inside the `for c in records:` loop, and `migrations/050_playbook_body.sql:77-78` confirms the trigger is still `FOR EACH STATEMENT`.** `XS ~15m · in `app/handlers/eudamed.py`, not in the playbook code` **Every EUDAMED certificate-register pull invalidates every worker's playbook cache once per matched actor, including for writes that change nothing.** `050`'s `manufacturer_epoch` trigger is `FOR EACH STATEMENT`, and a statement-level trigger fires whether or not it touched a row -- measured 2026-08-27: five consecutive `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` no-ops bumped `playbook_epoch` from 2 to 7 with the row count unchanged at 1. `handle_eudamed_certregister` runs `_MANUFACTURER_UPSERT` inside its per-actor loop (`app/handlers/eudamed.py:432`) to guarantee the `manufacturer_srn` FK target exists, so one register pull bumps the epoch N times and every worker re-reads all 38 playbooks on its next `load_playbooks()`. **Not a correctness bug** -- the cache is doing exactly what it was built to do, and the re-read is one indexed query -- but it is churn proportional to register size on a path with nothing to do with playbooks. Fix belongs in that handler, not in `app/playbooks.py`: hoist the upsert out of the loop and write the distinct canonicals once before it. Do not make the trigger row-level to dodge this -- statement-level is what keeps the epoch cheap for the writes that DO matter. Found while planning the merge back to master; recorded in the plan's "Merging back to master" section §4.

Measured on the live database 2026-08-27, both joins over the same data: against `manufacturer` **1 value / 3 groups**; against `manufacturer_name` (the 437 rows the seed actually writes) **4 values / 11 groups** -- `ULRICH STORZ GMBH & CO. KG` 5, `3SHAPE MEDICAL A/S` 3, `''` 2, `NORITAKE` 1. The second is what the report meant before 042. **This is not theoretical: it already happened.** On the 2026-08-27 deploy the report named only 3SHAPE while `playbooks sync` warned about 6 stranded groups the report could not see (`[orphaned-groups-ustomed-kuraray]`), and it is the standing visibility `[orphaned-groups-3shape]` and `[blank-manufacturer-vat-placeholders]` both assume they have. Fix is the one join. Give it a test that seeds a `manufacturer` row 042-style with no `manufacturer_name` behind it, or the next branch to touch this re-derives the same hole.

### Blocks or degrades the paid sweep

- [ ] 2026-08-27 [gate-calibration-map-absent] `M · split out of [gate-thresholds] when Denis closed the 0.92 question; needs S0.4 data` **Per-model confidence calibration is wired end to end and fed an empty map on every call.** Verified at the 2026-08-27 tree: `Config` (`app/config.py:513-533`) declares eighteen sections and `calibration` is not among them, so both call sites — `app/handlers/gate.py:856` and `:1019` (`:755`/`:861` when filed) — read `getattr(cfg, "calibration", {}) or {}` and always get `{}`. Everything downstream is real and just runs identity: `_score` (`gate.py:269`) takes the minimum of `tiers.calibrate(conf, model_id, field, calib_map)` over `REQUIRED_FIELDS`, and `_field_confidence` (`:276`) puts task 5's `validity_from` auto-file and §7b's `manufacturer` auto-bind through the same path deliberately, so that a model-tier swap cannot silently change what "confident" means. With an empty map it cannot, because nothing is being changed at all: a Haiku 0.93 and a Sonnet 0.93 are the same number to GATE. **This is what makes 0.92 a threshold on RAW model self-report.** That is a separate question from the value, which Denis settled 2026-08-27 and which is not reopened by this: a calibration map does not move the bar, it makes the confidences arriving at the bar comparable across tiers. Needs S0.4 measurement (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-13-calibrate-gate-thresholds.md`, 0 of 10 steps; `tools/calibration.py` was never written) — or a decision to record identity-calibration as deliberate and delete the parameter, which would at least stop the code implying a map it never receives. Gaps G5/G2.

### Pipeline — extract, validate, gate, resolve, discover, fetch

- [ ] 2026-09-09 [sds-exclusion-needle-was-wrong-in-three-places] `XS ~10m · two docstrings, and the number they quote` **Three places say NSK's safety data sheets are excluded by the needle `sds_`, and that needle reaches 13 of them.** `app/playbooks.py`'s `Crawl.doc_type_from` docstring, `app/handlers/discover.py::_excluded_by_type_rule`, and the `[crawl-doc-type-from-is-probe-only]` entry all quote `{"sds_": null}` and the figure "446 safety data sheets". Measured live 2026-09-09 across all 1329 harvested NSK links: the filenames are `NSK-SDS-001-DE-EU_Rev000.pdf`, so the underscore form matches **13**, while `sds` matches **444 of 444** SDS-labelled documents and **0 of 124** declarations. The count is also off — 444, not 446. The shipped recipe uses `sds` and a test pins it, so **nothing is broken**; what is left is that the two docstrings still teach the wrong needle to whoever authors the fourth recipe, and one of them is the function that does the excluding. Fix is to correct both quotes and the count. Filed while authoring the recipe (2026-09-09).
- [ ] 2026-09-09 [playbooks-drift-lies-when-run-in-the-worker] `S ~45m · a wrong answer, not a missing one` **`dentalia playbooks drift` run in the `worker` container compares the database against the playbook files BAKED INTO THE IMAGE, not the ones in the tree, and reports confident drift that does not exist.** Hit live 2026-09-09: immediately after authoring the NSK and Ultradent recipes it reported `1 file-stale, 1 diverged` with a `resolve in the editor` instruction, while the same command in the `test` container — which bind-mounts the repo — reported `38 in sync, 0 file-stale, 0 diverged` against the identical database. Confirmed the mechanism directly: `python -c "json.load(open('/app/playbooks/nsk.json'))"` shows `crawl: False` in `worker` and `crawl: True` in `web`, because `Dockerfile` COPYs `playbooks/` while compose bind-mounts `./playbooks:ro` into `web` only. **This is worse than a stale read**: the command exists to tell an operator whether the files and the rows agree, and in the container `docs/runbook.md` tells them to run it in (`docker compose run --rm worker python -m app.cli ...`) its answer is about neither the tree nor the deployment. It does NOT affect the pipeline — the worker reads playbook bodies from ROWS via `set_source`, never from those files, so only this one diagnostic is wrong. Options: bind-mount `./playbooks:ro` into `worker` the way `web` already has it (one compose line, and makes the answer true); or have `drift` refuse to run when it cannot see a bind-mounted directory, the way `playbook_drift` already renders nothing rather than guessing when the directory is absent. Prefer the second — a diagnostic that cannot see its input should say so, not answer anyway.
- [ ] 2026-09-07 [crawl-item-relation-is-a-product-family] `M ~half day · needs Denis's go` **The item-relation half of the 2026-09-04 ruling does not fire, because the key is wrong.** Measured on two live libraries: `ref matches: 0 of 1.329` (NSK) and `0 of 147` (Ultradent). The numbers in those anchors are the manufacturer's own DOCUMENT numbers (`000018-12-21-04_008`, `1013897AR01`), never our catalogue refs. What the anchors do carry is the **product family**, and so do our labels: measured with a throwaway regex, **31 of NSK's 136 groups** share a model-code token with some link, every sample match correct — `KOLENČNIK KIRURŠKI X-SG20L 20:1` ↔ `X-SG20L Ti-Max X-SG … Declaration`, `AIR SCALER TI-MAX S970KL` ↔ `S970L S970KL S970SL S970 Ti-Max`, `KOLENČNIK FX55M 4:1` ↔ `FX58m, FX55m, FX74m`. 22% is a floor: the regex also pulled `APR-2013` as a model, and most of the 105 misses are spare parts (BEARING, GEAR SET) with no declaration to match. **Proposal**: (a) the judge answers `product_names` beside `article_numbers` — same call, no extra cost, still once per library so migration 061's cache is untouched, and the model can tell a document number from a product name where a regex cannot; (b) the per-group match stays deterministic — intersect the group's `label` + `member_mfr_refs` tokens with the link's `product_names` + `article_numbers`, requiring a token of length ≥4 carrying both a letter and a digit, or an exact `mfr_ref` hit; (c) order becomes match → `TYPE_RANK` → confidence. Absence stays neutral, never a penalty. **Limit to state before building**: this improves ordering precision, not coverage, and it cannot link a document to an item — VALIDATE's REF gate still does that from the document's own content.

- [x] 2026-09-04 [crawl-ai-link-triage] **DONE 2026-09-04, in three steps: a first cut, a correction, then caching (migration 061).** The first cut reused the search ranker's one score, which on a manufacturer's own library put a clearly-titled IFU beside a DoC and never looked at article numbers. Now a T1 link judge answers type + article numbers per link, once per library version (`crawl_link_rank`); the order is code — our-article-match, then DoC > EC > ISO > IFU > other, then confidence — for any collection of five or more links, never a filter. **Not yet measured**, and that is the one thing left: `python -m tools.crawl_judge_dryrun --index-url ... --link-pattern ... --refs ...` (added 2026-09-04) judges a live library page and prints the type mix, the ref matches and what the budget would keep versus page order, writing nothing. Run it on NSK (1.329 links, page text labels 160 DoC / 446 SDS / 22 certificate) and Ultradent (147 IFUs) before authoring either recipe — NSK is harvestable again: `[crawl-harvest-data-href]` closed the same day, so `harvest` reads its `<tr data-href>` rows and the withdrawal recorded in `playbooks/nsk.json`'s portal note is itself now stale — re-probe before trusting either note. Costs about nine Haiku batches for NSK, one for Ultradent, well under a dollar. **MEASURED LIVE 2026-09-07 on nsk-library.com** (`tools.crawl_judge_dryrun`, no writes): 1.329 links judged in 27 batches for **$0,479** — 129 DoC, 6 ISO, 2 EC, 578 IFU, 614 other. A 200-link budget spent by judgement keeps **129 declarations and zero `other`**; page order keeps 118 and **60 junk**. That is the ruling's payoff, and it is paid once per library version, not once per group — NSK's 136 groups share the one $0,479. Two defects the run exposed, both fixed 2026-09-07: a batch of 150 needed ~8,2k output tokens against an 8k ceiling, so one batch in four truncated and 150 links silently became `other` (batches are 50 now, ceiling 16k, and a batch answered with nothing raises rather than degrading); and `crawl_rank_max` was 600 against NSK's 1.329, so the largest library we hold tripped `pattern-too-broad` and fell back to page order — the exact behaviour the ruling replaced (1.500 now, `capped_at_ceiling` went 729 → 0).

- [ ] 2026-08-14 [filed-relink] `M ~2h · RULED 2026-08-27: shape (a), the scheduled scan. Buildable now` **Denis chose the scan over the INGEST-contract change.** Reasoning on record: at today's size the scan is cheap and it changes no contract; the per-row INGEST lookup is the right answer only at a scale we are not at. **Re-measured the day of the ruling, which is what made it due: 443 filed documents, 423 of them carrying a `ref_list` in evidence** — up from the 162 that were on the books when it was deferred, and the 423 is the number that matters, because a filed document with no `ref_list` has nothing for a scan to match on. What to build: a scheduled job comparing filed documents' stored `ref_list` against recently mirrored `item_mirror` rows and re-emitting `validate.doc` for hits. Two things to get right rather than discover later — it re-scans the whole filed set each run (fine at 443, wasteful at tens of thousands, so revisit the shape then, do not pre-optimise now) and it can miss a window, so the scan must be by `item_mirror.updated_at` range with overlap rather than by "since last run". **This ruling and `[mfr-bind-empty-class]` interact:** that one now keeps more documents `staged` instead of `filed`, so the filed set this scan serves grows more slowly than it would have. Original entry: **Deferred deliberately by Denis 2026-08-14: "ship filed first, decide later."** A `filed` document (C15, migration 025) covers no item Dentalia currently stocks. Nothing brings it back when the catalogue gains one, so **until this exists a filed document stays filed even after its item is stocked** — that is the known, accepted cost of shipping the disposition first, and it is the whole reason this entry exists. Two shapes: (a) a scheduled scan comparing filed documents' stored `ref_list` against recently mirrored `item_mirror` rows — ~2h, no contract change, can miss a window and re-scans the whole filed set each run; (b) INGEST emits `validate.doc` for every filed document matching a newly mirrored item — exact and self-healing, but touches the INGEST contract and adds a per-row lookup at Zagreb's ~100k scale. Measure the filed-set size after the sweep before choosing: (a) is fine at hundreds, wasteful at tens of thousands.

The name was left alone deliberately — renaming touches `validate.py`, `gate.py` (CAPPING_FLAGS), `web/app.py` (FLAG_EXPLANATIONS), the PRD, the handbook, `vocabulary.md` and the tests, which is well past the 3-file rule for one commit. - [ ] 2026-08-17 [extract-not-idempotent-on-stored-attempts] `M ~2h · design call` `handle_extract_doc` runs the tier ladder unconditionally — there is no check against `extraction_attempt` for the same `(content_hash, extract_rev)`. So anything that re-emits `extract.doc` for content we have already parsed pays the full T1/T2 price again, and the only thing standing between a corpus re-scan and a repeat bill is `backfill._hash_seen`'s `fetch_log` lookup (see the entry above — one CASCADE from empty). Measured scale: 328 documents at the observed $0,041 each is ~$13 to re-derive what is already stored. Whether to skip, or to bump `extract_rev` and re-parse deliberately, is a design call: a re-parse is exactly what you WANT after a prompt or template change, so the guard cannot simply be "attempt exists". Probably `extract.doc` should carry an explicit `force` or target rev in its payload, making an accidental re-extraction impossible and a deliberate one obvious. Worth settling before the 985-PDF sweep, where the same slip costs ~$40.

- [ ] 2026-08-11 [backfill-not-regenerable] `S ~45m · runbook note alone is XS ~10m` **Deleting a pending `extract.doc` job strands its document permanently.** `backfill.scan` checks `_hash_seen` against `fetch_log` *before* enqueueing (`backfill.py:78`), and a backfilled document's ledger row is written in the same pass that emits its job — so once the job is gone, a re-scan counts the file as `seen` and never re-emits it. The module docstring's "re-scanning a corpus is ~free" is true and is exactly what causes this: the dedupe that makes re-scans cheap also makes job deletion irreversible. Deleting the `fetch_log` rows alongside the jobs restores regenerability (verified 2026-08-11: re-scan of the GC folder reports `scanned: 149, emitted: 87, seen: 62`), but that is only safe while no `document` row references them — `fetch_log.doc_id` is the check. Worth a guard or at least a note in the runbook before anyone prunes the queue on a corpus with real registry rows behind it.
- [ ] 2026-08-11 [worker-type-filter] `S ~45m` `queue.claim` takes a `types` filter (`queue.py:70`) that `runner.py:48` never passes, so a worker claims every pending tag. There is no way to run only the free tags (`validate.doc`, `gate.candidate` — neither makes an LLM call) while leaving paid `extract.doc`/`discover.group` work parked. Worked around on 2026-08-11 by emptying the queue of paid jobs instead; a `--types` flag (or `WORKER_TYPES` env) would make the free path runnable without queue surgery, and is useful beyond cost control (draining one stuck tag, isolating a handler under test).
- [ ] 2026-08-12 [queue-breadth-first-stalls-output] **RE-CHECKED 2026-09-02 — unchanged; `claim()`'s `ORDER BY priority, run_after` is now `app/queue.py:131` (was :86).** `M ~3h · migration` **The pipeline drains breadth-first, so nothing reaches the registry until the widest stage finishes.** No handler passes a priority when enqueueing the next stage, so every downstream job takes `queue.enqueue`'s `sweep` default, and `claim` orders `priority, run_after` (app/queue.py:86). During the GC pilot that meant all 148 `extract.doc` had to finish before the first `validate.doc` ran, and every validate before the first `gate.candidate` — and only GATE writes `document`, so the Documents page stayed empty for the whole run and would have filled only at the very end (Denis, 2026-08-12: "why am i not seeing documents"). Worked around live by bumping pending `validate.doc` to `delta` and `gate.candidate` to `interactive`, plus a loop re-bumping newly created ones; both are operator actions, not a fix. Do NOT fix by promoting sweep-born downstream jobs to `interactive` — priority classes mean work class (interactive = user-triggered, delta = incremental, sweep = bulk), and a backfill's gate job outranking a human's ingest is worse than the stall. The fix is to make claim ordering prefer later pipeline stages *within* a priority class (e.g. `ORDER BY priority, stage_rank, run_after` with a rank column or a CASE over `type`), which gives depth-first drain without corrupting what priority means. Two payoffs beyond visibility: results are reviewable while the sweep runs, and a bug in a late stage surfaces after document 1 instead of document 148 — on the full ~1,300-document corpus that is the difference between a cheap discovery and an expensive one.
- [ ] 2026-08-12 [validity-date-guard] **RE-CHECKED 2026-09-02 — this entry overclaims. Check (1) is live (`app/handlers/validate.py:993`) and so is the verbatim-phrase check (`:1060-1061`, `expiry-on-certless-doc`). **Check (2), a future-date guard, does not exist** — only a plausible-year-band test (`_YEAR_MIN <= d.year <= _YEAR_MAX`), which is a different guard. Check (3) is correctly absent (withdrawn).** `M ~3h · check (4) alone is S ~1h` **Deterministic guard on extracted dates, because the 2026-08-12 prompt fix is a request and not enforcement.** MDR Annex IV point 10 requires a *place and date of issue* on every DoC and there is no expiry element at all — a DoC stays valid while its device and cited certificate do. EC/ISO certificates are the opposite: MDR Annex XII gives them both a date of issue and an expiry, and Article 56 caps validity at five years. So the expected dates are a function of `document.type`, which we already extract: `DoC` → issue date only, `validity_to` should be null essentially always; `EC`/`ISO` → both. Three checks worth having, the first two lifted from EUDAMED's own certificate business rules: (1) `validity_to > validity_from` when both present; (2) `validity_from` not in the future; (3) a `validity_to` on a `DoC` is suspicious by type and should flag rather than store silently. A fourth, cheaper and specific to the bug seen: require the evidence `verbatim` for a date field to contain an explicit validity phrase, since all 7 correct extractions cited one ("Expiry Date", "Scadenza / Valid until", "Current Issue Date") and all 3 wrong ones cited a city. Note the confidence signal too — correct dates came in at 0.95-1.0, the three wrong at 0.75-0.85 — but 10 samples is far too thin to set a threshold on, so measure before tuning `cfg.gate.high` for date fields.
      **AMENDED 2026-08-13 -- check (3) is withdrawn, it was built on a false premise.** "A `validity_to` on a DoC is suspicious by type" is true of the regulation and false of this corpus: of 831 declarations in the SFTP folders, **251 carry an expiry phrase on page 1** and **181 have a real date beside it** (`END-DoC-Bioceramic Sealer-EN.pdf`: "issue date: 2020-03-23 expiry date: 2023-07-12"). The check would have flagged up to 181 legitimate documents. Not every hit is the declaration's OWN expiry -- some cite an ISO certificate's, and `EI-INS-DoC-DAC Premium Plus` dates its CE 0197 mark under the Pressure Equipment Directive -- so a type-conditional rule is still wanted, just not this one. Checks (1), (2) and the verbatim-phrase check stand. Blocked on client question 15 (`docs/2026-08-13-vprasanja-za-dentalio.md`; still unanswered in the 2026-08-18 round — they skipped it): whether the issue/signing date populates `validity_from`. **No longer a blocker as of 2026-08-13** — Denis ruled the signing date IS the start and it shipped (`app/extract/t0_templates.py:103`, `app/extract/tiers.py:54`); 178 of 306 declarations in the registry carry a start date as of 2026-08-17, up from 0 of 127. The client question is now ratification of that ruling.**

Live 2026-09-04: **68 IFU documents, 3 of them at `production`** — and DISCOVER now returns IFUs in bulk, which is the complaint in `[discover-should-rank-doc-ec-iso-above-ifu]` (the 09-03 slice: 49 IFUs against 26 DoCs out of 83 documents). That is false on the device side.** Verified 2026-08-20: `GET https://ec.europa.eu/tools/eudamed/api/devices/udiDiData?page=1&pageSize=25&size=25&iso2Code=en&languageIso2Code=en` returns JSON, no key and no login, reporting `totalElements` **3.196.505** across 127.861 pages, and **every record carries the manufacturer name AND the manufacturer SRN** (observed `CN-MF-000012832`, `GB-MF-000008558`, `SG-MF-000002111`) plus country, risk class and market status. `ec.europa.eu/robots.txt` does not disallow `/tools/`. The endpoint set is documented unofficially at `raw.githubusercontent.com/openregulatory/eudamed-api/master/eudamed_api_openapi_3_1.yml`. Separately, the DG SANTE developer portal at `developer.datalake.sante.service.ec.europa.eu/api-details` is real, public and has no robots.txt, but its catalogue is JS-rendered so **whether it publishes a bulk ACTOR dataset is UNCONFIRMED** -- do not repeat that claim as fact. Correct the research doc; it is the basis for `eudamed.sync` scoping.

(Fagnano Olona, X-Mind DC manual 07/2024 p.2), SOPRO S.A. (La Ciotat, PSPIX2 IFU 2024-08), plus PRODUITS DENTAIRES PIERRE ROLLAND (731720074) proven to exist in the group with a manufacturing NAF but not proven to sign products. The vendor-master name itself is NAF 46.18Z, a trade intermediary, and appears on **zero** device documents. Consequence: **REF-pair auto-write is unsafe for this code** -- invariant 3 requires the manufacturer established before any number is compared. `playbooks/acteon.json` therefore authors NO manufacturer alias on purpose (aliasing SATELEC onto a distributor's canonical name would attribute its documents to a trade intermediary); documents naming them will flag `manufacturer-unresolved`, which is safe but uncovered. The real fix is per-product-line identity, which one-code-per-supplier cannot express. Also unresolved: Satelec's MDR-era status -- every Satelec document found still cites MDD 93/42/EEC and carries no Basic UDI-DI. One 2024+ Satelec IFU citing 2017/745 would settle it; a paper IFU in a Dentalia carton would do it in seconds.

- [ ] 2026-08-20 [url-keyed-fetch-dedupe-breaks] `M ~2h · invariant 6` **Two independent manufacturers serve documents at URLs that change while the content does not**, so a fetch ledger keyed on the URL re-fetches every time. (1) Acteon's ungated `dl.acteongroup.com/qr/{id}` route 302s to a **presigned OVH S3 URL** carrying `X-Amz-Expires=3600` -- the signature differs per request. (2) SUN's Webflow CDN prefixes every asset with a 24-hex id that **changes on re-upload** (`65f1a28ed5938ab6840ad62e_IFU_ZP-SL_A.pdf`); the stable key is the trailing filename. Invariant 6 ("unchanged content never re-fetched") holds only if content-hash dedupe is carrying the load. Verify `app/urls.py` normalization and the `fetch_log` key against both shapes, and decide whether normalization should strip presigned query parameters and leading asset-id prefixes.

- [ ] 2026-07-06 [sftp-source] `L ~1 session · the adapter itself, with its three constraints` **MERGED 2026-08-27 (triage part b): the two `XS · constraint note for the adapter above` entries were never separate work.** They said so in their own effort tags, and holding them as rows made the backlog look three items deeper than it was. Build an SFTP SourceAdapter using **paramiko, not the OpenSSH `sftp` CLI** — the server (WeOnlyDo 2.7.0) reports malformed file-type/permission attributes that make `sftp get -r` silently refuse every file ("not a regular file"); paramiko's `listdir_attr`/`open` don't hit this. The adapter must also, all three measured against the live server on 2026-07-06 and none of them guesses: **(1)** force UTF-8 (`LANG`/`LC_ALL=en_US.UTF-8`, or read raw bytes) — the OpenSSH client mangles non-ASCII filenames (German umlauts, Slovenian diacritics) into literal octal-escape text under the default locale, producing unrecoverable-looking "missing file" failures that are an encoding bug and not missing data; **(2)** carry per-file retry/resume rather than one long-lived recursive transfer — the connection drops unpredictably after a handful of operations (observed 6-20 files per session, no pattern); **(3)** treat neither of those as optional hardening — each one on its own makes the transfer look like it found nothing.

Delete rows older than N× the beat interval on every write, or show age rather than presence on the status board.

Add the six data tables (not `schema_migrations`) and a seeds entry each; `docs/test-infra.md:159` corrected 2026-09-04.

The leak-on-kill mechanism and the missing sweep chore are both as filed.** `S ~45m` Per-run test databases (`dentalia_test_{pid}`) are dropped by the session fixture, but a run killed before teardown leaves its database behind — `dentalia_test_120448` survived one such kill. Harmless individually, but they accumulate. Add a sweep (drop `dentalia_test_%` whose pid is no longer alive) at session start, or a `cli` chore.
- [ ] 2026-08-14 [playbooks-sync-count-misleading] `S ~45m` **`playbooks sync` reported "0 inserted, 0 updated, 408 unchanged" in the same run that rewrote four rows.** Today's authorised sync flipped `manufacturer_alias.source` from `vendor-master` to `playbook` for codes 002/028/036/160 (verified in the registry before and after: `playbook` 26 -> 30, `vendor-master` 382 -> 378), while the operator-facing line said nothing changed. The counters evidently track canonical-NAME changes only, so a provenance-only rewrite is counted as `unchanged` — the one number an operator reads to decide whether a registry write happened is the number that misses it. Either count `source` flips in `updated`, or print them separately (`4 reprovenanced`). Same family as `[playbooks-orphan-delta]`: a sync count that is true of names and false of rows.
- [ ] 2026-08-13 [commit-absorbs-foreign-edit] **RE-CHECKED 2026-09-02 — the discipline is recorded (`tasks/lessons.md:623-649`) but the mechanisation it names as the actual differentiator — a script that refuses to run against a dirty target — **does not exist**; nothing like it is under `scripts/`.** `S ~30m · FOURTH occurrence 2026-08-27, and this one has a cause the process rule does not cover` **A 2026-08-27 commit absorbed two `tasks/followups.md` entries another session had written into the working tree — `[web-access-classes-undocumented]` and `[vendor-import-allow-renames-authority]`, neither present at the 2026-08-27 tree** — and committed them under a message about cert inheritance. Self-caught, nothing lost, authorship wrong. **The new part: the usual mitigation was unavailable.** "Stage only your own hunks" assumes hunks exist to choose between; that session had just run a script (`sweep.py`) that rewrote the whole file to move 41 entries into `## Done`, so by staging time every line was one diff and `git add -p` could not separate mine from theirs. **So the rule needs a second clause: before running any whole-file rewrite on a shared file, check `git status` and stop if it is dirty** — the check has to happen BEFORE the rewrite, not before the commit, because the rewrite is what destroys the evidence. That is mechanisable (a script that refuses to run on a dirty target), unlike the other three occurrences. Was: Third occurrence of one session's commit absorbing another's uncommitted file. A correction to `PHASES.md:268` (the reversed date rule) was written by one session and committed by another on 2026-08-13, in a commit whose message describes a gate flag change and says nothing about PHASES. The two earlier occurrences are written up in PHASES.md's pre-import-foundations incident note (two commits, both 2026-08-07), both of which likewise misdescribe their own contents. The cost is archaeological: `git log -- PHASES.md` attributes a doc correction to a gate commit, so the reason for a change is unfindable from its message. No code fix exists for this -- it is `git add -A` in a shared checkout -- so the mitigation is either per-session worktrees for anything but the file being edited, or committing a shared doc immediately after touching it rather than at session end.

There is no separate Zagreb, but the catalogue does grow to ~100k on the same numbering, so every figure below stands; read "ZG" as "the catalogue after it grows".)** **ZG-scale ingest is per-row + single mega-transaction.** `handle_ingest_run` does one `SELECT`-by-PK + up to one `UPSERT` + one enqueue per row, all in the worker's single tx. Proven fine for LJ (19,091 rows). At ZG (~100k) that's ~100k round-trips in one long transaction (WAL/lock/vacuum pressure; a late failure rolls back the whole run and retries ≤5×). Before S2.5, collapse to a set-based `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (item_ref) DO UPDATE SET … WHERE item_mirror.* IS DISTINCT FROM EXCLUDED.* RETURNING item_ref, mirror_rev` (changed rows == returned rows) + batched resolve.group enqueue. (Review flag, not a bug today.)

- [ ] 2026-08-18 [komet-sizeless-family-stubs] `M ~2h · design call` Komet prints one code per product row, and on 38 of its 186 corpus PDFs the size column is blank: `589.204.`, `NS0115.009.`, `9891.000.` -- 1.176 distinct, 2.521 occurrences, 18% of every row the text-column reader sees. The playbook pattern requires a size, so those rows contribute NO ref code at all (measured 2026-08-18: 13.667 codes with an empty size allowed, 11.146 without). Keeping them verbatim was measured and rejected for now: **zero** of the 1.176 equals any of the 629 Komet catalogue keys in either spelling, and exactly three are a prefix of one, so a family/prefix rule would fan a single stub across a whole size family -- the false link invariant 3 exists to prevent. What is open is whether those product rows should be reachable at all (ask Komet whether a sizeless list entry means the whole size family, and whether Dentalia stocks any of them), not whether to widen the regex. - [ ] 2026-08-27 [backresolve-writes-without-an-audit-row] **RE-CHECKED 2026-09-02 — unchanged. `_backresolve_citing_docs` (`app/handlers/gate.py:440-460`) still just returns a count, `_backresolve_here` is called at four sites (:852, :913, :933, :1107), and every one discards the return — no `_audit` call follows any of them.** `S ~1h · invariant 10` **`_backresolve_citing_docs` wrote `cert_doc_id` on 80 documents and left no audit trail.** Measured 2026-08-27 on job 35601: `SELECT * FROM audit_log WHERE via_job=35601` returns **zero rows**, yet that job changed the effective expiry of 80 documents, 32 of them `production`. The function (`app/handlers/gate.py:408`) returns the row count and every caller discards it; nothing calls `_audit`. Invariant 10 says the audit trail must stay fully interpretable with the `job` table deleted, and today the single largest registry change of the day is invisible to it. Two shapes: one audit row per back-resolved declaration (accurate, 80 rows for one job) or one summarising row on the certificate carrying the affected doc_ids in `detail` (cheaper, and `audit_log.detail` is jsonb for exactly this). Prefer the per-document row — `audit_log.doc_id` is what every reader filters on, and a document whose expiry changed should say so on its own page. Note the count is not hypothetical head-room: the 2026-08-27 fix makes back-resolution fire for every future certificate, so this stops being a one-off the moment the next EC certificate lands.
- [ ] 2026-08-19 [cert-number-match-ignores-the-manufacturer] **RE-CHECKED 2026-09-02 — the closing ask is already live. The revision-suffix normalisation shipped — `_RCODE_SUFFIX = re.compile(r"\s+R[0-9]+$")` plus a `regexp_replace` in the query, commented with Denis's 2026-08-19 ruling and the 80/306 figures. **The manufacturer-blindness half is untouched**: `_resolve_cited_certificate` still never reads its `manufacturer` argument.** `S ~1h · design call` **NEW EVIDENCE 2026-08-27, and it argues against the obvious fix.** Measured across all 175 candidate pairs on the live registry: no declaration matches two certificates, and none crosses companies — so the collision this entry fears has zero instances today. What it DOES have is the opposite failure. Doc 792's certificate carries manufacturer evidence `VOCO GmbH` while its 91 citing declarations carry `VOCO`, and `manufacturers.normalize` only lowercases (`'VOCO' -> 'voco'`, `'VOCO GmbH' -> 'voco gmbh'`), so **adding the manufacturer to the WHERE clause — or folding through `normalize` — would have blocked 91 correct links rather than preventing a wrong one.** That is the entry's own warning ("the fix is not simply adding the parameter to the WHERE") confirmed with numbers. Whatever lands must fold through the alias table the way `resolve_canonicals` does, not through string normalisation, and it should be measured against these 175 pairs before it ships. Original entry: `_resolve_cited_certificate(conn, fields, manufacturer)` accepts a manufacturer and never uses it: the query filters on `type IN ('EC','ISO')` and the cited number alone, across the whole registry. Two manufacturers issuing certificates whose numbers collide would cross-resolve, and a DoC would inherit an expiry from another company's certificate -- the shape invariant 3 exists to prevent on the REF side, unguarded here. Latent today at 17 certificates, and it was harmless while the match was exact-string; `[cert-citation-misses-by-a-revision-suffix]` loosened the match on 2026-08-19, which makes the collision surface larger and this worth closing before the corpus grows. The fix is not simply adding the parameter to the WHERE: a certificate's manufacturer comes from evidence, and the canonical fold has to be the one `manufacturers.normalize` applies, so it needs the same treatment the REF gate got rather than a raw string compare. **Cert inheritance has never once fired on real data, and one revision suffix is why.** Measured 2026-08-19 on the live registry: `cert_doc_id` is NULL on all 326 documents, so migration 020, the `inherited` branch of `document_effective_expiry` (027), `_backresolve_citing_docs`, and both web holes closed today (`[final-cert-status-join]`) address a population of ZERO. The cause is not missing data. 156 declarations carry no expiry of their own; **80 of them cite `MDR 778483`**, and we hold doc 138 -- an EC certificate, status `production`, GC EUROPE N.V., cert_number **`MDR 778483 R000`**, valid to **2030-04-16**. `_resolve_cited_certificate` compares cert numbers for equality, so the `R000` revision suffix makes it a miss. Those 80 declarations -- a quarter of the whole registry -- show no renewal date at all: only 28 of the 156 have an issue date, so the other 128 cannot even fall back to the five-year review horizon and render blank. The same shape appears on the other side: 96 declarations cite `G15 043306 0282 Rev. 00` while we hold `Rev. 01` (those 96 already carry their own `validity_to`, so nothing is lost there -- but it confirms the pattern is revisions, not typos). **The ruling needed before any code:** is `MDR 778483` the same certificate as `MDR 778483 R000` for the purpose of lending an expiry, and is `Rev. 00` the same as `Rev. 01` or a superseded predecessor? R000 reads as revision zero of the same certificate; `Rev. 00` vs `Rev. 01` reads as a supersession, which is a different answer. Getting this wrong writes a false renewal date onto 80 production declarations, so it is not a regex to widen on our own judgement. Only once ruled: normalize the revision suffix in `_resolve_cited_certificate` and re-run the back-resolution.
- [ ] 2026-08-21 [t0-doc-class-ignores-authored-type-markers] `XS ~15m · cosmetic` `classify_doc_class` decides `business-doc|msds|compliance-doc|unknown` from the GLOBAL `_TYPE_MARKERS` via `_has_compliance_marker` (`app/extract/t0_templates.py:441`, `:178` when filed), and a playbook's authored `type_markers` never reach it. Traced before filing: this cannot lose a document. The compliance-marker check runs AFTER the business and MSDS checks, so an authored marker could never rescue a document from either early exit, and `doc_class` gates nothing downstream except those two values (`app/handlers/extract.py:198`). The only effect is a recorded label: a document whose only type signal is an authored phrase reads `doc_class="unknown"` while its `type` reads DoC. Not trivially fixable either — `classify_doc_class` runs at `t0_templates.py:1402`, one line BEFORE the steering playbook is resolved, deliberately, because it is the cheap early exit. Decide: pass the playbook into the classifier (moves the resolve above the early exit), or document the asymmetry where `type_markers` is described. Inert until some playbook authors `type_markers`; none does.

- [ ] 2026-08-27 [migration-041-collides] `XS ~10m · convention only` The 2026-08-27 master merge left two migrations numbered 041: master's `041_vendor_import.sql` and this branch's `041_eudamed_job_types.sql`. Not a bug — `_migration_files` sorts on the full filename so eudamed runs first, both are pure additive `ALTER TYPE job_type ADD VALUE` with no dependency between them, and `schema_migrations` tracks by filename so an already-migrated database re-runs neither. Left alone deliberately: renumbering ours to 047-052 rewrites six files to fix a convention. - [ ] 2026-08-31 [docs-link-guard-reads-worktree] `XS ~5m · guard is blind to its own failure mode` `tests/test_docs_sets.py::test_no_broken_internal_links` resolves hrefs with `(f.parent / href).resolve().exists()` — a FILESYSTEM check, so an UNTRACKED file on the author's disk satisfies it. That is exactly how `[test-infra-doc-never-committed]` survived: green in the main checkout where the untracked file sat, red in every worktree, fresh clone and CI run — the one place the author would never look. The guard was added at 14:46 on 2026-08-31, three minutes after the commit that broke it, and passed. Fix is to check `git ls-files` membership rather than filesystem presence, so the test asserts what a fresh clone gets. Same class of bug as the guard being tested: validating the working tree instead of the commit.
- [ ] 2026-08-31 [test-container-oom-at-n4] `S · intermittent, costs a whole run` The full suite at `-n 4` intermittently OOM-kills an xdist worker inside the test container's `mem_limit: 4g` cgroup (`docker-compose.yml:442`). Observed 2026-08-31: `Memory cgroup out of memory: Killed process (python)`, `constraint=CONSTRAINT_MEMCG`, victim at 1.26 GB total-vm / 362 MB anon-rss, while the HOST still had ~6 GB free. **The failure mode is the expensive part: the killed worker becomes a zombie and the pytest controller waits on it forever — no timeout, no error, the run simply never returns.** Sat 13.5 minutes at 0.7% CPU before being killed by hand; `./scripts/test.sh --heavy` (`-n 2`) then passed 2739 in 223s. Not reproducible on demand — the same tree passed at `-n 4` twice earlier the same day, so it depends on which rasterisation-heavy tests share a worker. Options: raise `mem_limit`, cap worker memory, or make the default `-n` sensitive to the limit. Whatever else, the silent infinite wait deserves a timeout.

- [ ] 2026-08-26 [manufacturer-seed-is-one-shot] **RE-CHECKED 2026-09-02 — still open, and the newer CLI does not close it: `dentalia manufacturers seed` is idempotent but depends on `vendor_master` being populated, which is equally not automatic on a fresh environment. Migration 042:17-19 remains the only unconditional INSERT. Cross-referenced by today's `[no-bring-up-rehearsal]`.** `S ~30m` Migration 042 seeds `manufacturer` from `manufacturer_alias` **once**, at migrate time. Nothing populates `manufacturer_alias` in a migration — RESOLVE fills it at runtime — so a fresh environment migrates against an empty table and `manufacturer` stays empty permanently. It only produced 384 rows on the dev database because alias data already existed there. The EUDAMED work routes around this (its handlers upsert the row before writing `manufacturer_srn`), but anything else that assumes `manufacturer` is populated needs the same guard, or RESOLVE should keep the two in sync.
- [ ] 2026-08-26 [ec-iso-certs-unattributable] `M ~2h` **34 of the 43 EC/ISO certificates we hold (79%) cannot be attributed to any manufacturer** — they carry a `cert_number` but have no `item_document` at `status='production'`, so nothing connects them to an item, a group, or a `canonical_manufacturer`. Surfaced by the EUDAMED certificate-findings work, which needs that attribution to decide whose certificate a EUDAMED record corresponds to; only 9 documents reach `held_certificate`. Independent of EUDAMED: it means the registry holds notified-body certificates it cannot say anything about. Now counted and displayed as the `unattributable` coverage bucket on `/expiry` and the manufacturer page rather than dropped silently, but the underlying gap is unfixed. Worth asking why these 34 have no production link — never linked, or linked and later retracted?
- [ ] 2026-08-27 [udi-never-populated-blocks-article-matching] `L · needs a Dentalia data decision` **`item_mirror.udi` is empty on all 15.958 items (0,0%), and it is the only key that can join our catalogue to EUDAMED's register.** EUDAMED is keyed on UDI-DI and Basic UDI-DI. The column exists in `item_mirror` (migration 002) and nothing has ever written to it. Measured live 2026-08-27 after the first real register pull and sweep.
  Consequence: `eudamed_declaration_gap` joins `eudamed_mirror.reference = COALESCE(im.mfr_ref, im.item_ref)`, i.e. it falls back to comparing reference STRINGS, and those do not correspond. Demonstrated end to end on MODJAW: sweep succeeded and mirrored 8 devices whose EUDAMED references are `TWIM`, `FORKS`, `TALLY`, `TIARA`, `TIARA2`, `TIMAD`, `NAVEX`, `SMILIT`; our two MODJAW articles are `TIM10` and `TIM4` ("TECH IN MOTION FULL", one of them a DEMO unit) with `mfr_ref` empty. Same products, two unrelated naming systems. `eudamed_gap_summary` for MODJAW reads `covered=0 missing=0 staged=0 not_in_eudamed=2` — the `not_in_eudamed` bucket doing exactly the job it was built for, which is refusing to report an unmatched article as "no gap", but the useful output is empty.
  Why the fallback cannot be repaired in code: `mfr_ref` is set on 8.876 of 15.958 items but is IDENTICAL to `item_ref` on 7.450 of those, leaving 1.426 genuinely different, and some of those are free text rather than a reference (`1060/1 -> ne obstaja več!`). Denis, 2026-08-27: Dentalia is not accurate with that field and it cannot be relied on at all. So there is no ordering, preference or normalisation over `mfr_ref`/`item_ref` that makes this join work — the key is absent, not malformed. This also kills the article-probe SRN fallback for the same reason (see `[eudamed-probe-not-operator-reachable]`): it searches EUDAMED for one of our article numbers, which no manufacturer's register entry carries.
  **What is unaffected:** the certificate half of the feature keys on manufacturer NAME and CERTIFICATE NUMBER, both of which we hold reliably. The first live register pull mirrored 4.624 certificates and auto-attributed 28 manufacturers on exact normalised name matches (9 more queued as fuzzy). Revision drift, status alerts, certificate gaps and the coverage denominators are all real and unblocked. Only per-article declaration gaps are blocked.
  **The question for Dentalia, and it decides the size of the work:** is UDI held in Business Central and simply not exported, or was it never captured? An export change is small; a data-collection project across ~4.265 medical-device articles is not. Until one or the other happens, `eudamed_declaration_gap`, `eudamed_gap_summary` and the manufacturer-page gap panel will keep returning everything in `not_in_eudamed`, correctly and uselessly.
- [ ] 2026-08-27 [web-image-never-smoke-tested] **RE-CHECKED 2026-09-02 — the import-closure half is fully built (`tests/test_web_import_closure.py`, three tests). **No container smoke test exists** — nothing builds `Dockerfile.web`, starts it and requests routes; a grep for "smoke" across the repo returns nothing.** `S ~1h` **Nothing builds or runs `Dockerfile.web` outside a human loading a page.** The suite runs in the `test` image, built from `Dockerfile` with `.[extract,resolve,ingest,discover,fetch]`, so the slim web image's dependency closure is never exercised: a `web/` module acquiring a top-level import of a worker-only package is green in every test and broken in production. That is exactly what happened on 2026-08-27 -- `_parse` reached for `app.extract.tiers` and therefore PyMuPDF, and `/playbooks` reported all 38 playbooks unparseable with no reachable editor behind it. `tests/test_web_import_closure.py` now closes the import half by blocking the eight worker-only packages in a subprocess and importing every `web/` module, and it is the whole guard: it says nothing about anything else the image needs at runtime, e.g. a directory `Dockerfile.web` does not COPY (the `playbooks/` mount was one, resolved by moving the store into the database). A real smoke -- build the image, start it, request a handful of routes -- would cover the rest.

The RUNNER half landed a "Cross-cutting mechanics" section (`docs/dev/handlers.md:183-196`) covering four of the six asks; **domain-lease mechanics and wedged-vs-idle guidance are still missing**.** `M · Denis asked 2026-08-31` **No assembled doc for either long-running surface.** For the RUNNER: claim → dispatch → finish, backoff, dead-lettering, the `{"_deferred": True}` self-defer contract, the domain lease, the 300s visibility timeout, and how to read a wedged vs idle worker — all real, all scattered across `docs/code-map.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, the handbook and PRD §4, assembled nowhere. - [ ] 2026-08-31 [storage-local-root-relative] `XS ~5m · cosmetic, scoped` **Scoped 2026-08-31, and it is not a data-loss bug.** `worker` declares `STORAGE_LOCAL_ROOT=/archive` (verified with `printenv` in the running container) and logs the warning zero times, so archived bytes are on the `archive_data` volume as intended. The warning fires only in services that do NOT declare the var and never archive anything -- `test`, and now `scheduler`, which started emitting it on 2026-08-31 when it first ran as a normal compose service. Harmless, but it is the first line in the scheduler's log and reads like a fault. Fix by declaring `STORAGE_LOCAL_ROOT` on `scheduler` too, or by scoping the warning to processes that can actually archive.

- [ ] 2026-08-31 [scheduler-run-now-for-tick-crons] `S ~1h` **Five of the seven crons still have no Run now.** `web/scheduler_view.py` offers it only for `report.weekly` and `email.poll`, whose product IS a job. Since migration 054 a job type exists for the rest (`scheduler.tick`), but the obvious implementation is wrong: enqueuing a second `scheduler.tick` for a cron creates a SECOND perpetual job, because the handler always self-defers, and the dedupe key would have to differ for the insert to land at all. The correct shape is to pull the existing armed row forward -- `UPDATE job SET run_after = now() WHERE dedupe_key = 'scheduler.tick:{cron}' AND status = 'pending'` -- which creates nothing and works uniformly for all seven. `dentalia_api` already holds UPDATE on `job` (verified 2026-08-31), so no grant change is needed. Not done with the rest of the UI slice because it changes what "Run now" means for the two crons that already have it, and their 16 tests encode the enqueue semantics.

Also unbuilt: no `$filter` is ever constructed, so `delta_since` is inert and every OData run is a full catalogue read; when delta lands it must key on `systemModifiedAt`.
