- [ ] 2026-09-02 [suite-makes-live-llm-calls] `S ~1h · costs money on every full run` The test container carries a real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (108 chars, verified `docker compose exec -T test`), and nothing stops a test from reaching the live API. Two paths do it today: `tests/test_llm_schema_vocabulary.py::test_schema_is_accepted_by_the_live_api` is deliberate and self-documenting ("skipped without a key so CI stays free") but is NOT skipped here, because the key is present — so every `./scripts/test.sh` full run bills a Haiku call. The second was accidental and is now fixed: wiring `_default_rank` (`[discover-t1-ranking]`) turned `test_search_default_rank_degrades_to_manual` into a live call, found because it started failing rather than because anything guarded it. The general fix is an autouse conftest fixture that replaces `sys.modules["anthropic"]` with a client that constructs but raises on `messages.create`, plus a `@pytest.mark.live_llm` opt-out for the schema test (all `import anthropic` in app/ is lazy and inside functions, verified, so patching the module is sufficient). Touches `tests/conftest.py` (full-suite trigger), `tests/test_llm_schema_vocabulary.py`, and the marker registration.
- [ ] 2026-09-02 [gate-covers-only-backfill] `M ~3h · deliberately deferred` **`backfill.scan` is the one archive writer with no byte check.** Its two `store.put` sites (`app/handlers/backfill.py:358` the coverage-map index, `:398` the corpus PDFs) archive whatever the corpus folder holds. Everything else now shares `archiving.is_document`, and `email_poll` has its own wider `_is_pdf`. **Why it was left when the rest landed 2026-09-02:** adding the check fails **31 of 41 tests** in `tests/test_backfill_handler.py`, which write bodies like `b"AAAA"` and `b"BBBB"` into `.pdf` files on purpose — the file's stated property is that BACKFILL treats corpus bytes as opaque and lets EXTRACT decide. Closing this means rewriting those 31 fixtures to carry real magic bytes, which is a change of test intent across a whole file, not a guard. **Why it is low risk meanwhile:** the corpus is a trusted SFTP dump from Dentalia, not the open web, so there is no server answering `200 text/html` at a `.pdf` URL; and T0's header-window rule plus the doc-class call refuse a non-document before any `document` row exists, so the registry cannot be polluted either way. The exposure is a junk file sitting unreferenced in `/archive/<BRAND>/`. Do this one when the backfill tests are being touched for another reason, and change the fixtures in one deliberate pass rather than alongside a behaviour change.
- [ ] 2026-09-02 [scheduler-tick-is-dead-code] `M ~2h · found by Denis: "tests are not always testing the real code"` **`app/scheduler.py::tick()` is called by nothing in `app/` or `web/` -- only by `tests/test_scheduler.py`.** Verified 2026-09-02 by grepping every call site. The live path since migrations 054/055 is one `scheduler.tick` job per cron, dispatched from `CRONS` in `app/handlers/scheduler_tick.py` and armed by `runner.arm_crons`. `tick()` survived as an orchestrator nothing orchestrates, and it still LOOKS like the place a cron is registered -- which is exactly what went wrong: `coverage-scan` was added there alone that morning, passed the whole suite, printed as registered in a live worker's own config dump, and would never have fired. **The cron set now lives in five files** (`app/handlers/scheduler_tick.py` twice, as `CRONS` and `POLL_SECONDS`, plus `app/scheduler.py`, `web/scheduler_view.py`, `tests/test_scheduler_tick.py`, and historically `migrations/055_scheduler_tick_seed.sql`), so every guard can only check that the copies agree, never that a cron runs. Two drift guards landed the same day and they are a tourniquet, not the fix. The fix is to delete `tick()` and move its `_tick_*` tests onto the dispatch path, or make it derive from `CRONS` so there is one registry. Deleting is the honest option and wants Denis's nod first, since it removes a public-looking function and rewrites a test file.
- [ ] 2026-09-02 [tests-cover-unreachable-code] `M · the general case behind [scheduler-tick-is-dead-code]` **Nothing tells us when a test is the only caller of the code it tests.** `app/scheduler.py::tick()` is the proven instance: a green suite, a function production never calls, and a cron that could not fire. The suite cannot distinguish "this works" from "this works in a configuration nobody runs", and neither can a coverage report, which counts the test's own call. Worth one cheap sweep: for each public function in `app/` and `web/`, count call sites outside `tests/`; anything with zero is either dead or reached dynamically (handlers via `register()`, crons via a dispatch table, CLI entry points), and the dynamic cases are enumerable. Expect a handful of real finds plus a list of known-dynamic exceptions to pin. Not a framework -- a script, and a followup per find.
- [ ] 2026-09-03 [no-full-chain-test] `M ~4h · found by the readiness audit` **No test drives a document through the pipeline via the real queue.** Four tests named "end to end" each run ONE handler through `run_once` and then assert by SQL that the next job was *enqueued* -- they never claim or execute it (`test_resolve_handler.py:404`, `test_discover_handler.py:596`, `test_ingest_handler.py`, `test_email_poll_handler.py:497`). `fetch.url -> extract.doc` and `validate.doc -> gate.candidate` have no runner-loop coverage at all. The closest real chain, `test_verification_hooks.py::test_qms_cert_binding_path`, calls four handlers in sequence against a real PDF and real transactions, but by hand-stitched direct calls that bypass claim, dedupe, retry and dead-lettering, and it enters at `backfill.scan` so it never touches the web-discovery half. **What this cannot catch:** a payload field the next stage silently ignores, or a dedupe-key collision that only appears after several real hops. 2.443 tests, real Postgres, nothing mocked at the DB layer -- the suite is deep per handler and has never once watched a document travel. A `run_until_drained` helper plus one fixture chain would close it.
- [ ] 2026-09-03 [grouping-history-purged] `S ~1h · audit-trail nicety, NOT a coverage or correctness problem` **Narrowed 2026-09-14:** the fifth readiness audit filed this as "most of the catalogue has no record of how it was grouped" (F12) and that overstates it. `item_group_member.match_basis` records the decision for **every one of the 15.958 items** — 8.208 `singleton`, 7.750 `name-family` — which is the registry-derives-queue principle working as designed. What the pruned jobs took is the *evidence behind* a name-family match: the score and the runners-up. F12 is withdrawn; this entry stands at its true size. Original entry follows. **Roughly 10.000 grouped items have no surviving `resolve.group` job row explaining how they were grouped.** `item_group_member` holds 15.958 rows (every item), but only 5.919 distinct item refs appear in the recorded `resolve.group` results; `job` starts at id 16.901 with 2.521 ids missing inside the range. Invariant 10 says deleting every `job` row must leave the audit trail fully interpretable -- true for the registry, where `audit_log` is self-contained by design, but grouping is recorded in the registry only as its *result*, so "why is item X in group Y" is unanswerable for the bulk of the catalogue. Either accept it explicitly (grouping is regenerable: re-running RESOLVE reproduces it) and write that down next to the invariant, or record the grouping basis on `item_group_member` so it survives a queue prune. Found by the 2026-09-03 readiness audit; not urgent, but it is a stated invariant that the current data does not satisfy.

Two more gaps sit behind it: `handle_bc_push` passes the bare `item_ref` as the PATCH URL (`client.patch(item_ref, changed)`), and nothing sends `If-Match`, which BC's API pages normally require on PATCH. - [ ] 2026-07-24 [udi-group-key] `L · after the ruling` **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-27 (triage part c), and one premise elsewhere is now false: `eudamed_mirror` is NOT empty — it holds 591 rows.** The gap is narrower and more specific than "we have no UDIs". We hold plenty: `document.basic_udi_di` is set on **468 of 769** documents, and EUDAMED's own 591 rows carry theirs. What is missing is exactly one join — the UDI of a *Dentalia catalogue item*: `item_group.basic_udi_di` is set on **0 of 8.211** groups, because `LJ_CSV_PROFILE` (`app/adapters/source.py:147`) maps five columns and none of them is a UDI, so `resolve.py:179`'s `INSERT INTO item_group` has nothing to write. So the decision is where an item's UDI comes from: BC adds a column, or we derive it by matching `mfr_ref` against `eudamed_mirror` — which is what the EUDAMED branch's per-manufacturer sweep exists to produce. Worth leaving until that branch lands, rather than deciding it twice. Original entry: `item_group.basic_udi_di` has no writer anywhere — RESOLVE's `_by_basic_udi_di` rung and VALIDATE's UDI gate branch are dormant (consistent with S1.1 "udi always null"). Before S2.3 (EUDAMED) makes them live: decide the population source AND whether a Basic-UDI-DI match may grant production-eligible links to members who joined the group via name-family (invariant-3 trust bypass one hop out). **Denis 2026-09-11: use EUDAMED's UDIs only where the match is 100%.** Measured that day under trusted SRNs, raw equality of `eudamed_mirror.reference` to `mfr_ref`/`item_ref`: 3.299 items get exactly one Basic UDI-DI, none ambiguous; at group level 955 groups agree and 12 conflict. Yield: 152 new item links to 19 production DoCs, and only 2 medical-device items would gain a DoC they lack (`document.basic_udi_di` holds one value per document, so this undercounts DoCs that list several). The name-family half above is still open.

Original text: **RE-CHECKED 2026-09-02 — unchanged, and the drift is explained: the branch is now `app/handlers/validate.py:1009-1027` (was :771-790) because task 5's `superseded_by_doc_id` logic landed in between — which this entry anticipated. There is still no `==` branch; equal dates fall through with nothing set.** `S ~1h · after a tie-break ruling` **Two revisions of the same declaration issued on the SAME DAY both reach production, and neither supersedes the other.** `_validate` compares candidate `validity_from` against the current production document's (`app/handlers/validate.py:771-790`): null on either side flags `downgrade-uncomparable`, `<` flags `older-than-current`, `>` sets `supersedes`. **Equal falls through all three and sets nothing** — no flag, no chain pointer — so the candidate reaches production beside the document it should have replaced. Near-unreachable while `validity_from` held a stated validity-period START (0 of 127 declarations carried one); as of 2026-08-13 it holds the ISSUE date, and a corrected reissue of the same declaration is normally same-day. Surfaced by that change, not caused by it — the gap was always in the comparison. Needs a tie-break ruling before the GC rerun multiplies it: newer `extract_rev` wins, or the equal case flags for review. Do NOT default to last-write-wins silently — invariant 5 persists supersession at the production transition, so an unrecorded tie leaves the chain wrong rather than merely undecided.

The counts also differ (1215 vs 1238), so the two are not comparable at a glance. Nothing is wrong with the fixes -- three agents' work merged clean and passed on master -- but a verification claim from a worktree is weaker than the same words from the main checkout, and the difference is invisible unless someone asks. Until then, dispatch prompts should require agents to report skip counts WITH reasons, which is what surfaced this.

### Closed 2026-09-14 — the fifth readiness audit

- [x] 2026-09-14 [deploy-check-blind-to-web] **DONE 2026-09-14.** `app/version.py` hashes a third root, `web/` (`*.py`, `*.html`, `*.css`, `*.js`; binary assets under `web/static` excluded). `_SOURCES` now carries several patterns per root, since a template-only change is a change the user sees. `scripts/deploy.sh` compares it, treats `ABSENT` as legitimate for a root an image does not carry (no `migrations/` in the web image, no `web/` in the worker's `app` stage) and adds `UNKNOWN` — an image whose own `version.py` never heard of a root predates it, so it is stale rather than waved through. Proven on the stale image before the fix (`<-- STALE app/`, `web=ABSENT`), then `./scripts/deploy.sh` rebuilt, migrated, restarted and verified all three roots. Five tests in `test_version.py`, including one that pins the root list itself. `/missing` answers 200 live.
- [x] 2026-09-14 [bc-push-preview-cert-join-unscoped] **DONE 2026-09-14.** The bulk preview no longer has a query of its own: `app/handlers/bc_push.py` exports `HOLDINGS_FOR_MANY_SQL` from the same `_HOLDINGS_SELECT` the stage uses, so the SRN-scoped, revision-aware certificate join is shared and only the WHERE clause differs. Four tests in `test_bc_web.py`, three of which failed on the old code; the fourth asserts the preview and the push compute the same fields for one item, which is the property that outlives either query. Was: 55 of 123 production documents with a `cert_number` matched a foreign `actor_srn` in the preview, touching 640 production-linked items.
- [x] 2026-09-14 [missing-documents-unreachable] **DONE 2026-09-14.** `/missing` shipped 2026-09-11 with no sidebar entry and no place on the `/pipeline` hub, so it was reachable only by typing the URL. Original entry: The NEODENT backfill linked **38 items of a measured 339**. Two independent caps make the LLM path structurally incapable on this corpus, and neither is a bug: `MAX_VISION_PAGES = 4` (`app/extract/llm.py:44`) means the 26-page T-bases declaration was read from its first 4 pages only, and `REF_LIST_PROMPT_CAP = 150` caps any answer at 150 refs against that document's 948. The model reported the truncation itself, in the stored evidence: "the source document is 26 pages long, so additional REF codes likely appear on pages not provided". The fix is the T0 template deliberately deferred until this evidence existed: the table is a clean repeating `Item | Description | GMDN Code` with a header on every page and no rotation, so `ref_strategy: "text-column"` with `header_anchors: ["item","description","gmdn code"]` and `field_pattern: \d{3}\.\d{3,4}` should enumerate it deterministically, exempt from the 150 cap. Related: `[ref-list-cap-cannot-cover-the-tail]`, closed 2026-08-14, which flags exactly this shape. **Re-measured 2026-09-11:** doc 521's stored text layer (26 pages, 66.933 chars) holds 948 distinct codes; 280 of our NEODENT catalogue items are among them and 38 are linked. `ref_strategy: "text-column"` already exists (`app/extract/t0_layout.py:32`, KOMET uses it) and `playbooks/neodent.json` has no template, so this is a playbook entry plus a re-extract, not code. Thirteen other model-produced REF lists sit at or near the cap and are suspects for the same treatment: NEODENT 519, 520, 522; IVOCLAR 18, 72, 170, 224, 272; KOMET 398, 399; no manufacturer bound 813, 894, 986. **Template authored 2026-09-11, NOT live yet.** `playbooks/neodent.json` carries `match` (`jjgc indústria` / `jjgc industria`), `ref_strategy: text-column`, header `item`+`description`, `field_pattern` `\d{3}\.\d{3,4}(?:-[A-Z]{1,3})?`; tests pin 947 codes off the committed fixture. Dry run on the live archive: 521 reads 947 (279 catalogue items, 38 linked today), 519/522 read 144 (59 items, one declaration twice), 520 reads 155 codes all suffixed `-ET` (0 exact matches; 79 if the suffix were dropped, which is NOT done: `-ET` may be a different product). Saving it into `manufacturer.body` was first refused over an unrelated playbook (`[futura-dental-blocks-every-playbook-save]`, fixed); it is live as NEODENT rev 1 (`ops:neodent-ref-template`). `repair-ref-list`, limited to 521 and 520, appended a new extract_rev for both (3 for 521): 520 filed again (`no-ref-overlap`, every code `-ET`), and 521 matched all 279 catalogue items, but GATE held the 241 new links `staged` behind manual task #470 because VALIDATE compared 521 with itself (`[revalidation-compared-a-document-with-itself]`, fixed 2026-09-11). **To land them:** redeploy the worker, then re-emit 521's `validate.doc` at extract_rev 3, or approve #470. 519/522 already store the same 144 codes the template reads and still link nothing, which is a separate question. The other ten near-cap lists have different layouts and need their own look; the NEODENT template does not reach them.

- [x] 2026-09-09 [iso-27001-is-an-iso-standard-the-rule-does-not-name] **CLOSED 2026-09-09 — ruled by Denis the same day: a non-medical-device ISO certificate is `other`.** The reasoning is scope, not wording: this registry holds medical-device compliance evidence, and a corporate certificate about something else is not that, whatever standards body issued it. Both prompts now say `ISO` means **ISO 13485 or ISO 9001 specifically** and name 27001 / 14001 / 45001 as `other` — `t1_text.md` and `t2_vision.md` together, because the vision prompt carried the identical gap and fixing one would have left the tier that reads scanned documents still conflating. `_ISO_STANDARD_NUMBERS` deliberately NOT widened: accepting 27001 would have silenced the flag on exactly the document it correctly caught. Doc 209 re-extracted for **$0,0022** and is now `other`; `validity_from`, `validity_to`, `cert_number`, `coverage_scope`, `canonical_manufacturer` and `status` all unchanged. **Swept for others the new rule would re-type: none.** No settled document typed `ISO` names an ISO/IEC standard other than 13485 or 9001, so the population was exactly the one document. Original entry follows. **Doc 209 is an ISO/IEC 27001 information-security certificate, and the prompt and the flag disagree about what it is.** Found by running the type backfill 2026-09-09: 248, 260 and 316 corrected `ISO` -> `EC`, and 209 stayed `ISO` through a fresh T1 call on the fixed prompt. T1 is not obviously wrong — `app/extract/prompts/t1_text.md:36-39` says `ISO` is "a certificate issued against the **ISO standard itself**", and 27001 IS an ISO standard; the clause that would exclude it is the next half-sentence, "it always names the standard number: ISO 13485 or ISO 9001", which reads as a description of the corpus rather than as a closed set. `tiers._ISO_STANDARD_NUMBERS = ("13485", "9001")` treats it as closed, so `iso-without-standard-number` DOES fire on 209 — verified directly against its stored fields 2026-09-09. So the two halves disagree, and the document sits at `staged` typed `ISO` with a flag saying nothing supports that. **The substantive question is not the wording, it is scope**: an information-security management certificate is not medical-device compliance evidence at all — it belongs to `other`, the way `[completeness-card]`'s requirement matrix treats non-MD documents. A 9001 certificate raises the same question one step milder. Cheapest close once ruled: state in the prompt that `ISO` means a QMS certificate against 13485 or 9001 specifically and that any other ISO/IEC standard is `other`, then re-extract 209 alone (~$0,002). Do NOT widen `_ISO_STANDARD_NUMBERS` to accept 27001 — that would silence the flag on exactly the document it correctly caught.

  ```python
  # docker compose exec -T test python3 -  (swap DENSTPLY for NEODENT / VOCO)
  import pathlib, warnings; warnings.filterwarnings("ignore")
  from app.extract import t0_layout, t0_templates as t0, pdf as pdfutil
  import pymupdf
  root = pathlib.Path("imports/dentalia-sftp/DENSTPLY")
  allrefs = set()
  for p in sorted(root.rglob("*")):
      if p.suffix.lower() != ".pdf": continue
      try: doc = pymupdf.open(p)
      except Exception: continue
      tpl = t0_layout.match(pdfutil.first_page_text(doc)); doc.close()
      if not tpl: continue
      allrefs |= set((t0.extract_ref_list(p, template=tpl) or {}).get("value") or [])
  print(len(allrefs))
  ```

  **The lesson is the gate, not the tables.** A verification script whose red state is normal stops being read: `deploy.sh --check` had been reporting NOT VERIFIED since 2026-09-04 on schema drift that was nobody's migration. Scratch belongs in a scratch database or a `TEMP` table, never in `dentalia`. Approved by Denis 2026-09-09.

The flag has therefore never been raised in production on a real case. Same shape as `[expiry-flag-should-be-about-evidence]`: the rule is right and the wiring cannot reach it. Fix is to move `flags.extend(tiers.integrity_flags(fields))` above the mfr-binding return so BOTH routes carry extraction-shape flags — `ref-list-possibly-truncated` is equally unreachable there today — with a test that seeds a manufacturer-scope `ISO` document naming no standard number and asserts the flag on the emitted candidate. Nothing in `docs/runbook.md` or CLAUDE.md tells you to rebuild, and CLAUDE.md's test section actively says the opposite for the container it is talking about ("the repo is bind-mounted, so rebuild by hand only when the Dockerfile or the dependency set changes") -- true of `test`, false of `worker`. (2) **Playbooks are DATABASE-authoritative and there is no CLI path to push a hand-edited file in.** The worker reads bodies from `manufacturer.body` (`app/workers/runner.py:237` `set_source`), not from `playbooks/*.json`. After authoring `edenta.json`, `dentalia playbooks sync` reported only alias counts and wrote no body; `dentalia playbooks drift` classified the file as **DIVERGED** and said "resolve in the editor", and `--apply` would have regenerated the file FROM the database, silently discarding the new recipe. The only route in was calling `web.registry.save_playbook_body` by hand. Fix is two things: say the rebuild requirement in the runbook (and stop CLAUDE.md's bind-mount sentence reading as global), and give `playbooks sync` a documented file-to-database direction -- or, better, close it with the probe/wizard (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-playbook-probe-wizard-design.md`), which exists precisely so a recipe is authored where it is stored. Cross-referenced by `[no-bring-up-rehearsal]` and `[playbooks-sync-count-misleading]`. **HALF CLOSED 2026-09-03.** Part (1), the rebuild gap, is addressed: `scripts/deploy.sh` builds, migrates, restarts and then VERIFIES -- each running image's per-root source digest against the tree's, then `schema-drift` against the database -- exiting 1 with `DEPLOY NOT VERIFIED` on any disagreement, and `docs/runbook.md` now says plainly that `docker compose up -d` does not pick up your code. Confirmed on the spot: before the deploy both `worker` and `web` reported stale; after it, both match. **Part (2) is untouched and still open** -- playbooks remain database-authoritative with no CLI path to push a hand-edited file in, `playbooks sync` still writes no body, and `--apply` would still regenerate the file FROM the database. The probe/wizard is still the proposed close. Do not read this entry as done.
- [x] 2026-09-04 [queue-timeout-fix-never-reached-the-worker] **CLOSED 2026-09-09 — fixed 2026-09-04, and this entry outlived it by five days.** `app/workers/runner.py:70` now calls `queue.claim(conn, worker_id, visibility_timeout_s=queue_cfg.visibility_timeout_s)`, and the runner takes `queue_cfg`, defaulting to `load_config().queue`. Verified at HEAD 2026-09-09: the live worker reclaims at the configured 1800s, not `app/queue.py`'s function default of 300. The function default stays 300 on purpose — it is a library default, and the caller that matters now passes the config. Original entry follows. **The 2026-09-02 fix for the sweep that rolled back 38 pages has never been in effect.** `app/config.py:421` declares `visibility_timeout_s: int = 1800`, raised from 300 precisely because job 35679 was reclaimed at 301s and lost everything it had stored. `app/queue.py:112-117` declares the same parameter as a function default of **300**, and `app/workers/runner.py:57` calls `queue.claim(conn, worker_id)` passing neither — the only claim call site outside tests. So the live worker still reclaims at 300s. This is the concrete, behaviour-changing instance of `[dead-config-keys]`'s "all four `queue.*` keys are shadowed": the other three happen to match their defaults, this one does not. Fix is to thread `cfg.queue` into the runner's claim and fail call sites; the test that would have caught it asserts on `queue.claim`'s signature, not on what the runner passes.
- [x] 2026-09-04 [claude-md-names-a-deleted-scheduler-service] **CLOSED 2026-09-09 — fixed, verified at HEAD.** `CLAUDE.md:67` now reads "Services: postgres, migrate, worker (replicas), web, caddy, test. **There is no `scheduler` service.** The standalone loop was deleted 2026-09-02 and the crons run ON THE QUEUE as ten perpetual, self-deferring `scheduler.tick` jobs", and the repo-layout block describes `app/scheduler.py` as "cron bodies; called by the scheduler.tick handler, not by a loop". No compose profile `full` and no `worker/scheduler image` phrasing survive. Original entry follows. **`CLAUDE.md:65` still lists a `scheduler` service under compose profile `full`.** No such service exists — `docker compose config --services` returns postgres, migrate, web, caddy, worker, and `docker-compose.yml:4-9` records that the standalone loop was deleted 2026-09-02 in favour of eight self-deferring `scheduler.tick` jobs claimed by the worker. Line 74's `app/scheduler.py` description ("cron loop") and line 90's "worker/scheduler image" read the same way. The file is the first thing every agent session reads, so a stale architecture claim there is expensive.

### Closed 2026-09-04 — the UI audit's own findings, worked in order

- [x] 2026-09-04 [probe-browser-tier] **CLOSED 2026-09-04 — built.** Escalates on the SAME trigger the crawl rung uses (§4.2): zero ANCHORS, never zero matches. A page with anchors that matched none has a wrong `link_pattern`, and a browser renders the same anchors and matches none again -- 30s to reprint the same number; a page with NO anchors is the one that might be a JavaScript shell. NOTE the entry's own example, "5 links, 0 matched", is therefore NOT the case this fixes and never should have been: that is a wrong pattern, not a missing browser. A render fault degrades to the static answer and `tier` stays `static`; a render that succeeds but finds nothing reports `rendered`, because "we tried" differs from "we never tried". `_probe.html` has read `probe.tier` since S2.2 and can finally print "Read with a browser". Four tests. Original entry follows: [probe-browser-tier] `S ~1h` `app/handlers/playbook_probe.py` hardcodes `tier="static"` and never escalates to the renderer. The crawl design's rung escalates on ZERO anchors; the probe does not, so a JavaScript-drawn library reports "5 links, 0 matched" rather than "this page needs a browser". `playbook_probe.tier` exists precisely to make "this portal needs a browser" measured rather than authored, and today it can only ever say `static`. Blocked on nothing; deferred because it is a second real fetch per probe and the spec (§4.4) prices it at ~30s.
- [x] 2026-09-04 [date-label-not-adjacent-not-computable] **CLOSED 2026-09-04 — STALE: this was built on 2026-09-03 and the entry was written as though it had not been.** Both halves the entry calls unresolved are resolved in the tree. (1) Not computable: `t0_templates.DATE_LABEL_REFUSED_KEY` records the refusal at the time it happens (`t0_templates.py:863`), which is exactly the "extractor emitting the refusal" the entry asks for, and `tiers.integrity_flags` reads it (`tiers.py:293`). (2) The conflation: `lost = [f for f in refused if f not in out]` -- the flag fires ONLY where the field went unset, never where a later label recovered it (doc 317). Denis chose that reading on 2026-09-04 ("flag only the lost field"). Pinned by `test_t0.py:1818-1841` and `test_tiers.py:544`. Nothing to decide and nothing to build. Original entry follows: [date-label-not-adjacent-not-computable] `S ~1h to decide, M to build` The second of the two flags in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-label-adjacency-and-type-conflation-design.md` §5 was never built, and the spec understates what it costs. `iso-without-standard-number` shipped because `integrity_flags(fields)` can compute it from the STORED fields. `date-label-not-adjacent` cannot: the adjacency rule REFUSES a pairing and falls through to the next label, so a refusal leaves nothing in `fields` — it is a non-event, invisible downstream. Building it means the extractor emitting the refusal (a counter or a field on the attempt row), not a VALIDATE-side computation. Also unresolved: the flag conflates "refused and recovered from a later label" (doc 317, correct behaviour) with "refused and the field went unset" (the case worth a `t0_layout` template). Decide which of the two is actually wanted before building either.

The four that stayed are not machine operation and burying them would have been the regression my first proposal contained: `/` is the site root and KPI board, `/reports` a compliance output, `/upload` takes `group_id`/`manual_task_id`, `/import` is where `/ingest` now sends people. A test asserts each half -- on the hub AND off the menu, plus the four that stayed. Folding is a placement, not a permission. **The real fix is still user roles**, which is a bigger question than a menu and is NOT closed by this. Original entry follows: `S ~2h` The sidebar carried 26 entries in three groups -- Registry 13, Correspondence 2, Pipeline 10, plus Developer. - [x] 2026-09-03 [crawl-defer-refetches-earlier-recipes] **CLOSED 2026-09-03 by the commit "crawl: one domain lease per host, not per recipe"; moved here 2026-09-04.** Verified rather than taken on the entry's word: `app/handlers/discover.py:938-939` takes every host's lease before any recipe fetches, and both pinning tests exist (`tests/test_discover_crawl.py:699`, `:736`). It sat in `## Open` because "FIXED" was typed into the effort-tag slot instead of the checkbox. **The standing lesson is not lost by closing this** — it is written out in full in `tasks/lessons.md` (§ "a green suite proved nothing about concurrency between two units the tests only ever exercised one at a time"), which cites this entry by slug, and a slug resolves here as well as it did above the line. **A per-recipe domain lease made a multi-library crawl re-fetch its first library forever, and only a live run showed it.** Edenta authors two crawl recipes on ONE host (`edenta.com`): instructions for use, and ISO/EC certificates. Recipe 1 took the lease and fetched; recipe 2 found that same lease held seconds later; the rung deferred the WHOLE job (a deliberate choice made that morning, so a deferred library would not be silently dropped); the retry re-ran recipe 1 **from the top** -- fetch, harvest, emit 0 because all 13 links were already active. **56 live fetches of one manufacturer's document list** before it was stopped by hand (`job 36428`, marked dead). Spaced by the 2s politeness interval so not abusive, but unbounded. **The test suite could not have caught this**: every crawl test used a single recipe, and the defect needs two recipes sharing a host. Fixed by taking ONE lease per host for the whole crawl, before any recipe fetches -- deferring there is safe because nothing has been fetched, which is exactly the property the loop destroyed -- plus an internal `politeness_ms` pause between recipes, since a multi-recipe crawl is one job making several requests and FETCH's per-job spacing does not apply. The lease now also precedes the `robots.txt` read, because that is itself a request to a host we have been asked to wait on. Pinned by `test_two_recipes_on_one_host_do_not_deadlock_each_other` and `test_recipes_on_one_host_are_spaced_by_the_politeness_interval`. **Standing lesson, and the reason this entry is kept: a green suite proved nothing about concurrency between two units the tests only ever exercised one at a time.**

Showing the three is what removed the need to choose. **What survives and is NOT closed:** `/coverage`'s three numbers and `_kpi_board`'s single headline still answer to different definitions, so the KPI board and `/api/kpi` can still disagree with the gap lists. That is `[kpi-coverage-denominator]`'s scope, not a separate item -- do not re-open this one as a build task.

### Closed 2026-09-04 — found by verifying every open claim against the tree, the live database and git

_Three entries were open only because nothing had re-read them. Each was fixed by work that closed a different entry, or by a commit that never named this one. The eight entries that were half-true were corrected in place above rather than moved._

- [x] 2026-08-25 [effective-expiry-inherited-never-fires] **CLOSED 2026-09-04 — the first of this entry's two readings was right, and it was fixed on 2026-08-27 without anyone closing the entry.** Re-measured live rather than read: `document_effective_expiry` now returns `staleness` 365, `stated` 279 and **`inherited` 80** over 724 documents carrying an expiry, all 80 with `inherited=true`. The cause was the citation-resolution gap this entry named as the interesting reading, not the too-narrow condition: back-resolution was unreachable code, a 2026-08-27 change fixed it, and one re-gated job took `document_effective_expiry.basis='inherited'` from 0 to 80 — the same numbers `[cert-citation-misses-by-a-revision-suffix]` records on its own closure. The third rung of the expiry ladder has executed on real data. Original entry follows: `S ~1h · measure, then decide` **`document_effective_expiry.inherited` is false for every document in the registry — 602 of 602 with a non-null expiry** (360 filed, 140 production, 97 staged, 4 superseded, 1 rejected; measured 2026-08-25). The view computes it as `d.validity_to IS NULL AND c.validity_to IS NOT NULL`, and `basis` has a matching `'inherited'` branch, so a whole third of the expiry ladder has never executed on real data — only in `tests/test_report_handler.py::test_expiring_documents_includes_docs_inheriting_a_cert_expiry`, which constructs the case by hand. Live `basis` is only ever `stated` (91) or `staleness` (44). Two readings and they need different fixes: either no declaration in the corpus cites a certificate whose expiry resolves (a RESOLVE/citation gap, and the interesting one — the 2026-08-07 commit built cited-certificate resolution precisely for this), or the condition is too narrow because a DoC that states its own `validity_to` can never inherit even when the certificate it cites lapses first. `app/handlers/report.py`'s docstring presents `inherited` as one of three live grounds; on this registry it is documentation of an intention. Measure which reading holds before touching the view.
- [x] 2026-08-18 [ingest-page-copy-is-false] **CLOSED 2026-09-04 — fixed by the commit "web: the ingest form no longer says the import does nothing".** `web/templates/ingest.html` now says what the form is (the developer path) and points at the page a person should use; it carries a comment recording the replaced sentence and naming its successor, `[ingest-page-says-the-import-does-nothing]`, which is open and is about retiring the page altogether. Original entry follows: `XS ~5m · UI` `web/templates/ingest.html:7` tells the operator "Parsing itself is a later stage -- the no-op worker currently just drains this job to done." That has not been true since S1.1: `app/handlers/ingest.py` parses the BC export, upserts `item_mirror`, scrubs prose refs, counts anomalies and fans out one `resolve.group` per changed item. A live page describing the system as a stub is worse than no copy.

### Swept 2026-09-04 — ticked `[x]` under Open or Waiting on a ruling, moved here verbatim

_Forty-four entries were ticked done but still sat above this line, so a naive `[ ]`/`[x]` count of Open overstated it (readiness audit 2026-09-04 §10). Moved unchanged, order preserved; nothing was re-verified in the move. One of them, `[migration-041-collision]` (the ruling copy), says "kept open as the note the merger reads" and is ticked anyway — the tick was honoured._

- [x] 2026-09-03 [cert-views-drifted-from-the-tree] `CLOSED 2026-09-03 -- data fixed by migration 058, guard shipped as `dentalia schema-drift`` **A migration edited in place after it was applied leaves every long-lived database serving the old definition, and NOTHING anywhere reports it -- not the tests, which build from scratch and are green, and not the migration runner, which keys on filename.** Found 2026-09-03 while investigating why `/expiry` served 1.43 MiB. `043_eudamed_cert_views.sql` was applied here 2026-08-26 10:28 UTC; a later commit edited that same file 2026-08-27 10:37 UTC, adding `SELECT DISTINCT` to `certificate_drift_candidate` and digit-normalised revision comparison to `certificate_drift`. `schema_migrations` is keyed on `filename`, so the edit never re-ran and this database served the pre-fix views for **8 days**. `tests/test_eudamed_views.py::test_certificate_drift_candidate_is_distinct_per_bound_article` pins the corrected shape and passes -- it could never catch this, because the defect is not in the tree, it is the gap between the tree and a database. **How it was found, which is the reusable part:** dump `md5(pg_get_viewdef(oid, true))` for every view in the live database and in a leftover pytest database (which is built from the current migrations), then join on name and compare. Exactly two views differed; every other view matched. **Fixed** by `058_cert_view_drift_repair.sql`, which recreates both from 043's current text: `certificate_drift_candidate` 5.650 rows -> 4, `/expiry` 1.43 MiB -> 37.966 bytes, and the whole-view diff is now empty. **What is still open is the guard, not the data.** A scan of `schema_migrations` against `git log` flags 26 applied migrations edited afterwards, but most are comment churn and it cannot tell which edits were semantic -- it is a lead generator, not a check. The real fix is to record a content hash at apply time (`schema_migrations.sha256`, written by `app/db.py`) and a CLI check that reports any applied migration whose file no longer hashes to what was recorded. That is a schema change plus a CLI subcommand plus a runbook line, hence the estimate. Related: `[worker-image-and-playbooks-are-stale-to-the-tree]`, which is the same class -- the tree is not the running system -- for code and playbooks rather than schema. **GUARD BUILT, and NOT the way this entry proposed.** The proposal was a `schema_migrations.sha256` recorded at apply time. Checked before building and rejected on three counts: it sees only ONE cause (a file edited after it was applied); it **cannot be backfilled honestly** -- hashing the files as they stand today would record 043's CORRECTED text against a database serving the OLD view, laundering the exact bug that motivated the work; and it is blind to a hand-run `ALTER`, a partly-restored dump, or an object made directly in psql. Built instead as `app/schema_drift.py` + `python -m app.cli schema-drift`: build the tree's schema in a scratch database, fingerprint both catalogues per object keyed by `(kind, name)` -- columns, views, indexes, constraints, enums, grants -- compare, drop the scratch. ~10s, exit 1 on drift. **It earned itself on the first run**, reporting three `tmp_*` tables (`tmp_ds`, `tmp_neo_refs`, `tmp_voco`, 0 rows each) created by hand in the live database that no migration describes and that a hash scheme could never have seen. Left in place, not dropped -- they are not mine to remove; **someone should confirm they are dead and drop them.** Also recorded: `pg_dump --schema-only | diff` is NOT a usable substitute, measured at ~100 lines of difference between two IDENTICAL schemas because it orders by dependency and stamps a per-session `\restrict` token. Documented in `docs/runbook.md` (§ schema-drift) and `docs/dev/changing-things.md`.

- [x] 2026-09-03 [source-fingerprint-is-meaningless-on-the-web-image] `FIXED 2026-09-03 same day` **`source_fingerprint` is the repo's stale-image detector and it CANNOT be used on the `web` image: it reports a mismatch every single time, stale or not.** It hashes `app/**/*.py` plus `migrations/*.sql` (`app/version.py` `_SOURCES`), but `Dockerfile.web` COPYs only `app/` and `web/` -- no `migrations/`, and not the whole of `app/`. Measured 2026-09-03: **71 files hashed inside the web container against 132 in the tree** (app 71 vs 73, migrations absent vs 59), giving `7d1c0d5dd8bd` on web against `022ebba11371` on host, worker and test. The two numbers are not comparable and never will be, so the documented procedure -- "run it on the host and in the image and compare, different values mean the image is stale" -- yields a permanent false positive for the one image serving the UI. `version.py`'s own docstring names this exact failure: "a fingerprint that changes for a typo is one people learn to ignore". `python -m app.cli version` also does not run there at all (`ModuleNotFoundError: pymupdf`, pulled in via `app.extract.t0_layout` on the CLI import path), so the documented command is doubly unusable and the fingerprint is only reachable by importing `app.version` directly. **Fix is a choice, not a patch:** either scope the fingerprint to what an image actually contains (hash the files present, and report WHICH set was hashed alongside the digest, so two numbers are only ever compared like with like), or give the web image its own fingerprint over `app/` + `web/` and stop pretending one number covers both images. Related: `[worker-image-and-playbooks-are-stale-to-the-tree]`. **Noted while confirming that one: the worker image was current only because ANOTHER session rebuilt it 8 minutes earlier** -- nothing in the deploy or the runbook makes that happen, so `docker compose exec worker python -m app.cli schema-drift` working today is luck, not process. **FIXED.** `version.fingerprint_parts()` reports a digest per source root, and `cli version` prints them, so `app` is compared against `app` and a root the image does not carry reads `not in this image` instead of silently poisoning one combined number. The combined digest is unchanged and still logged by the worker. **The blind spot was hiding a real defect**: measured the same day, the running web image was genuinely two files behind the tree -- `app/schema_drift.py` and `app/handlers/playbook_probe.py` -- and the combined number could not have told anyone, because it differs permanently for the unrelated reason that `migrations/` is absent. Also corrected above: the 33-vs-93 figures in the original entry came from a non-recursive glob and were wrong; the real counts are 71 vs 132. Now enforced by `scripts/deploy.sh`, which compares per root and refuses to report a verified deploy otherwise.
- [x] 2026-09-02 [no-bring-up-rehearsal] `CLOSED 2026-09-03 — and it found two real bring-up defects on its first runs` **Every piece of the deploy sequence is tested and the sequence itself is not.** Nothing — no test, script, or runbook procedure — takes an empty machine through: stack up, migrations, seed, ingest a real BC export, scan a corpus folder, assert the registry landed. `tests/test_compose_config.py` guards config drift and `test_web_import_closure.py` guards the slim image, but neither runs the order. That order is exactly what next week's deploy performs, and two known traps live inside it: `[manufacturer-seed-is-one-shot]` (a fresh environment migrates against an empty `manufacturer_alias` and `manufacturer` stays empty forever) and the archive/`archive_url` path rewrite, which has already needed a repair CLI once. Cheapest form is a documented runbook rehearsal against a throwaway database plus a smoke assertion, not a new test harness. **BUILT as `scripts/bringup-rehearsal.sh`**, in the cheap form this entry asked for: a throwaway database, the real sequence, smoke assertions, no new harness. **It paid for itself immediately, twice.** (1) `manufacturers seed --apply` **hard-fails on an empty database** -- `ForeignKeyViolation: Key (code_source, code)=(LJ, 001) is not present in table "vendor_master"`, because `manufacturer_bc_code` is FK'd to `vendor_master`. The BC vendor master must be imported FIRST and nothing anywhere said so; every test seeds `vendor_master` in a fixture, and the live database was populated in an order nobody wrote down. (2) The seed writes **no aliases at all** -- 381 manufacturers, 0 `manufacturer_alias` rows -- because aliases are `playbooks sync`'s job. A deploy stopping after the seed would report success at every step and then resolve nothing, leaving every group unnamed. That is `[manufacturer-seed-is-one-shot]`'s trap in its real form, sharper than the entry predicted. The verified order is now a table in `docs/runbook.md`. **Passes end to end**: 390 vendor codes, 381 manufacturers, 445 aliases, 15.958 items, 4.265 md_flag, 273 groups all named, no failed or dead jobs. **Still not covered** and deliberately so: no live fetching, so the archive/`archive_url` rewrite -- this entry's other named trap -- remains covered only by its repair CLI. A second lesson worth keeping: the script's own first draft asserted the SYMPTOM ("item_mirror is empty") before the CAUSE (a failed job), and reported the wrong thing for a path misconfiguration the job had already recorded; it now checks job status first.

  `S · blocks the EUDAMED merge, decide before either branch lands` **Two migrations claim 041, on two different branches, and neither one errors.** Verified 2026-08-27: master holds `migrations/041_vendor_import.sql`; `worktree-eudamed-enhancement` holds `migrations/041_eudamed_job_types.sql` (branched off a master whose tip was 040) and 042-045 behind it. `app/db.py:44` is `sorted(glob("*.sql"))` and `run_migrations` tracks by `path.name`, so after a merge BOTH files apply and nothing complains -- but `041_eudamed_job_types` sorts alphabetically BEFORE `041_vendor_import`, so a fresh database applies them in the opposite order to the dev database, where `041_vendor_import` already ran and is already in `schema_migrations`. That is a silent divergence between "the schema a new environment gets" and "the schema we have been testing against", which is the one thing a numbered-migration convention exists to prevent. **It does not stop there:** renumbering EUDAMED to 042-046 then collides with `playbooks-into-db`, which already took **046** (`046_manufacturer_entity.sql`) and 047 -- that branch correctly reserved 042-045 for EUDAMED and assumed EUDAMED would keep 041. So the renumber has to be decided across all three branches at once, not on whichever merges first. Precedent exists and is cheap: `worktree-agent-aa85e6f3850460794` did exactly this ("migrations: renumber the lineage migration to 037"). **Also worth deciding while here:** whether `_migration_files` should refuse a duplicate numeric prefix outright rather than sorting through it -- a four-line guard that turns this class of collision from silent into loud, and there is no test for it today. Found by the 2026-08-27 drift check. **Cost so far, from `playbooks-into-db`:** that branch renumbered its own migration FOUR times (040 -> 041 -> 042 -> 043 -> 046) as other worktrees took each number in turn, and 043 ended up claimed twice anyway -- the renumber is not a one-off tax, it is paid again every time a neighbouring branch lands first.

Note for the next re-check: this entry, `PHASES.md`'s own verified-against-the-code table and `tasks/todo.md` all still said OPEN on 2026-09-04, a day after the fix landed — and the commit that re-asserted it was a DESCENDANT of the commit that closed it. Was: **RE-CHECKED 2026-09-02 — unchanged, lines drifted (615→746, 701→847, 814→1023). `_is_md_document` is still called only from `_handle_mfr_binding`; the REF branch at `app/handlers/gate.py:1023-1024` never calls it, and no regulation or device-scope flag exists in BLOCKING/CAPPING/INFORMATIONAL.** `S ~1h · code, do NOT wait on the PPWR scope ruling` **`_is_md_document` guards ONE of the two paths to production, and the REF path is the unguarded one.** Verified at HEAD: `_is_md_document` (`app/handlers/gate.py:615`) has exactly one call site, `app/handlers/gate.py:701`, inside `_handle_mfr_binding` -- its own docstring says "Applied ONLY to the machine binding path". The REF branch, `disposition = "production" if score >= cfg.gate.high and ref_gate and not _gates_disposition(flags)` (`app/handlers/gate.py:814`), never consults it, and no VALIDATE flag covers regime: the whole vocabulary (`no-item-identifier`, `multi-group-match`, `manufacturer-unresolved`, `no-ref-overlap`, `ref-catalogue`, `multi-manufacturer-ref`, `ref-unscoped`, `date-insane`, `downgrade-uncomparable`, `older-than-current`, `auto-superseded`, `expiry-on-certless-doc`, `cert-unresolved`) is about REF matching, dates, supersession and certificates, never about whether the document is evidence of a device at all. So the 2026-08-17 PrograMill fix stops a non-device document being bound to a whole catalogue and does nothing to stop one that NAMES matching article numbers being written to those items individually. **No longer hypothetical -- measured on real files 2026-08-25.** GC's 980 PPWR packaging declarations (see `[gc-ppwr-archive]`) run through `t0_extract` return `doc_class=compliance-doc`, `type='DoC'` at **confidence 1.0**, `manufacturer='GC EUROPE N.V.'` at **0.97** (resolves to our canonical), `validity_from='2026-07-29'` at 0.95, and NO regulation -- which `_ESCALATE_ALWAYS` sends to T1, whose schema admits only `MDR|MDD|n.a.`, so they land as `DoC / n.a.` and are well-formed device declarations to every downstream stage. They declare a BOX (component tables: barcode label, cardboard box, bottle, cap, tray insert, with gram weights), and 868 of the 980 are for class IIa devices, which is exactly why they read as device DoCs. Fix, either shape: extend `_is_md_document` to the REF branch, or add a VALIDATE capping flag for a compliance document whose regulation is outside `MD_REGULATIONS` and whose type is not in `QMS_TYPES_WITHOUT_REGULATION`. Note it would ALSO cap the ~13 non-device documents `_is_md_document`'s docstring already names (cosmetics 1223/2009, LVD, EMC, machinery, RoHS) -- intended. Do not gate this on the scope ruling: these files reach us through the SFTP dump whether or not anyone decides to adopt PPWR. **CORRECTION 2026-08-26, from the full audit (`docs/2026-08-26-ppwr-audit.md`): the GC PPWR documents do NOT currently reach the REF branch, so they land at `staged`, not `production`.** `t0_extract` finds no REF list, so `derive_coverage_scope` falls back to `manufacturer` at 0.6, and `needs_escalation` suppresses `ref_list`/`basic_udi_di` on that VALUE rather than its confidence (`app/extract/tiers.py:131-133`) -- so no REF list is ever produced and they route to `_handle_mfr_binding`, where BOTH `scope_stated` (0.60 < `cfg.gate.high` 0.92) and `_is_md_document` refuse. The gap in this entry is still REAL and still worth closing -- GATE's REF branch has no device check and the docstring admits it -- but the urgency was mine, not the evidence's. What makes it worth doing anyway: `coverage_scope` IS escalated and `needs_escalation` is recomputed after T1 (`tiers.py:304`), so a T1 answer of `group` (the natural reading of a document that names one article) reopens `ref_list` at T2 and the REF branch with it. That single T1 answer is unmeasured and costs ~$0.001 to settle -- audit §6.1, and the top item in this week's `tasks/todo.md` block.

Original report below.
  `S ~30m · runbook + a dump list` **A registry wipe silently takes `fetch_log` with it, and `fetch_log` is the one table that makes a re-derivation free.** `TRUNCATE document, item_document, evidence, manual_task, audit_log RESTART IDENTITY CASCADE` also empties `fetch_log`, because `fetch_log_doc_id_fk` references `document` — measured 2026-08-17 during the option-B rebuild: 330 rows gone, having been explicitly listed as KEEP moments earlier. Recovered at no cost only because `imports/` was still on disk and the ledger is derivable from the files (`url_normalized` = `str(path.resolve())`, `content_hash` = `sha256(bytes)`, `source='backfill'`); a re-hash of 330 PDFs reproduced 330 rows / 328 distinct hashes exactly, covering all 328 stored extractions. Two things to do: state in the runbook that any registry reset must `pg_dump` `fetch_log` alongside the five registry tables (or drop the CASCADE and clear `fetch_log.doc_id` first), and note that CASCADE's reach is `document`'s inbound FKs, which is not the list a person writing the TRUNCATE is thinking about. Related: `[backfill-not-regenerable]`, which is the same ledger seen from the other side — there, keeping `fetch_log` while deleting jobs is what strands a document; here, losing it is what would have cost a re-extraction.

Root cause: that file builds `MSDS_PDF`/`BUSINESS_PDF` as module-level constants via pymupdf `tobytes()`, which embeds a wall-clock-dependent random `/ID` in the PDF trailer. The bytes are then used as parametrize arguments with no `ids=`, so the implicit test IDs differ between workers that import the module at slightly different instants. Hit twice in one session. Fix: pass explicit `ids=` to that parametrize, or build the PDFs in a fixture rather than at module scope.

After migration 058: **4 rows, `/expiry` 37.966 bytes, a 97,5% reduction.** Pagination is not needed on this board at its real size and is not being added. **The lesson worth keeping: the entry was re-checked twice and both re-checks measured the symptom (no LIMIT in the code) instead of the data (how many rows the view actually returns, and how many of them are distinct).   `S ~30m · reproduced 3x, cause still not established` **`tests/test_db.py`'s three `scratch_db` tests failed twice, then passed, and I could not find out why.** The fixture creates a database with the FIXED name `dentalia_test_migration_order` (`tests/test_db.py:143`), doing `DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS ... WITH (FORCE)` then `CREATE DATABASE` -- two statements, not atomic. Two runs in the scheduler-onto-queue worktree hit `UniqueViolation: Key (datname)=(dentalia_test_migration_order) already exists` at fixture setup, plus one `AdminShutdown: terminating connection due to administrator command`, which is what `WITH (FORCE)` does to another session's connection. That pair means two users of the same fixed-name database at once. **Checked afterwards and found nothing:** no pytest process in the container, no `dentalia_test%` database left on the cluster, no connections to it; the next full run was clean at 2682 passed. The worktree has its own compose project and its own Postgres, so a cross-worktree collision should be impossible, and `--dist loadfile` keeps the whole file on one xdist worker, so an intra-file race should be too. Both of those "should"s are now suspect. **Seen three times now (2026-08-31), with a different subset of the three tests each time**, so it is a live flake rather than a one-off -- and `test_db.py` is the only file that touches this name, so `--dist loadfile` should already be serialising them. Note the fixture does NOT take the cluster-shared advisory lock conftest uses for its own create-and-migrate (`tests/conftest.py:308-333`), which is the difference worth looking at first. Worth either a per-worker suffix on the name (the shared test DB already uses one -- `dentalia_test_26_580ed717`) or an advisory lock around the drop/create pair, the way conftest already serialises its own create-and-migrate.

- [x] 2026-08-17 [dentsply-ih-is-three-entities] **VERIFIED DONE 2026-09-02** — no defect exists to fix. `extract_manufacturer` (`app/extract/t0_templates.py:1135`) matches only full authored legal names, never a prefix, and its docstring states the exact DENTSPLY constraint this item worried about. Only `"DENTSPLY IH Inc."` is authored (`playbooks/dentsply-sirona.json:11`); no bare `Dentsply IH` prefix rule exists anywhere. Original report below.
  `XS · a name rule, not a client question` **`Dentsply IH` is three legal entities with two different roles, and treating the string as "not a manufacturer" would discard real ones.** Measured 2026-08-17 over all 1.303 readable corpus PDFs (PyMuPDF, first 4 pages): `Dentsply IH Limited` (Stonehouse, UK) appears in **41** files, in all 41 preceded by "Authorized UK-representative" — the post-Brexit UKRP, irrelevant to EU/MDR attribution, and every one of those declarations names its actual manufacturer on the same page (`Maillefer Instruments Holding Sàrl`, `Dentsply DeTrey GmbH`, with SRN). But `DENTSPLY IH AB` (Mölndal, SE) and `DENTSPLY IH Inc.` (Waltham, US) appear in **4 files each as certificate holders / production sites** on EC and QMS certificates. So any exclusion must match the full entity name, never the `Dentsply IH` prefix. This was question 4 of `docs/2026-08-13-vprasanja-za-dentalio.md`, **deleted 2026-08-17**: it claimed 66 files and claimed they would be left without a manufacturer, both false, and the roles are printed on the documents, so it was never Dentalia's to answer.
- [x] 2026-08-19 [t0-type-markers-confuse-subject-with-class] **VERIFIED DONE 2026-09-02** — all four proposed fixes shipped. `"quality management system"` is gone from `_TYPE_MARKERS` (`app/extract/t0_templates.py:61` carries the comment naming doc 420), `"eu certificate"` sits beside `"ec certificate"` (:72-73), `iso 13485` is the sole ISO signal, and confidence is position-scaled — `conf = 1.0 if i < _TYPE_TITLE_CHARS else _TYPE_MENTION_CONF` (:525). The item's "Not built" framing was the stale part. Original report below.
  `M ~3h` **Seven documents across five manufacturers are typed `ISO` from the phrase `quality management system`, and every one is wrong. Doc 420 is at `production`.** 248/260 (3SHAPE `MDR CE cert ...`), 420 (KOMET `BRASS_HZ ..._CVE_...`, production), 439 (KOMET `533213_Certificate-CE_MDR...`), 518 (NEODENT `neodent certifikat.pdf`), 316 (STRAUMANN `Certifikat Straumann (vsadki) MDR`) are all EC/EU certificates; 657 (VOCO `Declaration Art 22_VisCalor Dispenser`) is a declaration. Root cause: `_TYPE_MARKERS` conflates a document's SUBJECT with its CLASS. Doc 420's title is literally "EU Certificate Quality Management System" -- the phrase is real, at position 15, and the document is genuinely about a QMS; it is still a certificate. Two aggravating mechanics, both measured: the scan returns the first marker in LIST order found anywhere (`t0_templates.py:198`), so `quality management system` (index 10) beat `ec certificate` (index 11) at position 0 on doc 518; and a MENTION is read as an identity, so an IFU citing a DoC at character 33633 was typed DoC at conf 1.0 (NEODENT doc 520). `conf=1.0` means "an exact phrase matched", never "the type is certain" -- and the filename tier, which only runs when the text tier finds nothing, typed all four NEODENT documents CORRECTLY at 0.6. **Three fixes tested against the real files, 2026-08-19:** removing the marker fixes 6 of 7 and breaks 420 into `IFU@1.0` off boilerplate; position-wins alone fixes only 518; **drop `quality management system`, add `eu certificate` beside `ec certificate`, keep `iso 13485` as the only ISO signal, earliest-in-document wins, confidence scaled by position -> 7 of 7 correct**, with the four spot-checked ISO 13485 certificates staying ISO. Not built: it changes shared extraction logic and re-types a production document. Interacts with `_TYPE_SETTLED_CONF` (`t0_templates.py:348`), which gates the `group` deduction on type confidence, and with invariant 5, which supersedes on `(coverage subject, type, regulation)` -- a mistyped document can only ever chain against the wrong things.

Measured over the 4.265 confirmed MD items: any paper 4.254 (99,7%), device-regulation paper 1.684 (39,5%), a declaration of any age 1.461 (34,3%), an UNEXPIRED declaration **489 (11,5%)**. 11,5% is the honest figure, since lapsed evidence does not count as coverage (Denis, 2026-08-24); the gap to 34,3% is the 972-device expired-declaration pile `[completeness-card]` tracks, and PHASES.md quotes 34,3% only because it ignores expiry. Was: `M ~4h · defect inside DELIVERED scope, not new work` **The coverage KPI is reported on two delivered surfaces and the headline number is not defensible in front of the client.** `_kpi_board` (`web/app.py:1644`) feeds BOTH the status page and the read API `GET /api/kpi` (`web/app.py:3886-3889`) -- so it sits on both delivered surfaces, the web UI and the read API. Filed 2026-09-03 after a review briefly mis-shelved it as separate work; it is a **defect fix** and belongs in the build as new scope. The substance is PHASES.md `What is next` item 1, re-stated here because the defect register is where a defect belongs: 99,7% of devices are `covered`, **36,4% carry an article-level match**, **34,3% hold an actual Declaration of Conformity**, and **62,3% hold nothing but an ISO/QMS certificate** -- a claim about the manufacturer's quality system, not conformity evidence for the device. That is C16 working as ruled (one certificate, 2.567 Carl Martin items), which is exactly why a surface reporting 99,7% overstates. `_kpi_coverage` (`web/app.py:1476-1499`) already varies BOTH axes deliberately -- strict vs processed-scope denominator, any-paper vs device numerator -- so the machinery is there and the missing cut is **by document type**: a DoC denominator alongside the any-paper one, on the page and in the JSON, with the ISO/QMS-only population named rather than folded in. `docs/specs/kpi.md` needs the same row, and `docs/specs/read-api.md` is additive-only so the API gains a field and loses none. Do NOT ship a number to a client-facing report before this lands -- the 2026-08-31 delivery report already had to quote article-level coverage and explain why, which is the workaround, not the fix. **Amended 2026-09-03, after `/coverage` shipped and `[w11-no-doc-list-needs-one-definition]` was superseded into this entry.** Two things this entry did not say and must. (a) **It now has a second surface to reconcile with**: `GET /coverage` publishes three gap counts over the same `md_flag` population, and whoever builds the DoC cut here has to land on numbers that can be explained next to that page. (b) **The disagreement is on TWO axes, not one.** The doc-type axis is the one described above. The second is expiry: `_COVERAGE_HELD` does not filter `p.expires` at all, while `_kpi_coverage`'s ANY_PAPER/DEVICE_PAPER require `p.expires IS NULL OR p.expires >= CURRENT_DATE`. So `/coverage` counts an expired Declaration of Conformity as held and the KPI board does not. Measured 2026-09-03 over 4.265 `md_flag` items: **2.804 hold no DoC at all, 3.776 hold no UNEXPIRED DoC -- a 972-item gap**, driven by 20+ REF-list DoCs sharing `expires = 2026-05-04`. Decide the expiry rule explicitly, do not let it fall out of whichever query is copied.

PREMIUM 100KOS HS` drops. Full suite green: 2746 passed, 5 skipped. **Live Brave query, one call, real production data** (`INSTITUT STRAUMANN AG`, group 80, label `STRAUMANN VELIKA KASETA ZA BL IN TL`): new query `INSTITUT STRAUMANN AG STRAUMANN VELIKA KASETA ZA BL IN TL declaration of conformity pdf` (no quotes) returned **10 candidates**, several genuine straumann.com PDFs. **Out of scope, unchanged:** the T1 ranker (`_default_rank`) is still unwired (`[discover-t1-ranking]`) — the search rung now produces candidates instead of zero, but nothing downstream of the query fetches or ranks them yet; `DISCOVER_HOLD` and job enqueueing were not touched. Was: `S ~1h · needs one ruling · found by running it` **DISCOVER's search query exact-matches Dentalia's own Slovene BC description, so it returns zero for every manufacturer, always.** `_query_for` (`app/handlers/discover.py:219`) builds `{manufacturer} "{label}" declaration of conformity pdf` and `facts.label` is the internal catalogue text (`ROKAVICE L NEPUD. PREMIUM 100KOS HS`, `SVEDER T63 - 1,5 / 6,0 MM`). The quotes make it an exact-phrase requirement against text that exists nowhere on the open web. **Measured live against Brave 2026-09-02** over six manufacturers holding no documents (HENRY SCHEIN, INSTITUT STRAUMANN AG, NEODENT, IMES-ICORE, INTERDENT, CEFLA): **0 results each**. The identical query with the quoted label removed returns 10 results with 5-7 direct PDFs, including a genuine `EU_Medical_Device` certificate on `straumann.com`. **This stacks in front of `[discover-t1-ranking]` and changes its priority:** the query returns an empty candidate list before the unwired ranker is ever reached, so wiring the ranker alone would have achieved nothing, which was the plan of record (S1.7 P3). It also explains why `discovery_log` has never held a single `search` row. Fix is small but needs a ruling on which: drop the quotes, drop the label for Slovene-labelled groups, or normalise the label to a manufacturer product name. Worth noting the label-free query is manufacturer-wide, which is exactly the granularity `mfr-scope` evidence wants.

A per-type budget map would change the outcome for one document that is already extracted and paid for ($0,4155, one-off). **The correctness half cannot fire either, which is the part worth recording** — head-only truncation only bites a document over 360k, so no certificate has ever lost its tail, and `validity_to`-on-the-last-page-of-an-annex is a hypothesis rather than a defect; IFUs additionally stopped chasing dates on 2026-08-18, so the window cannot cost a date there. **Reopen when, not if:** any NON-IFU document exceeds ~150.000 chars, or a new corpus lands (DENSTPLY's 405 unscanned PDFs is the only candidate on record and `[denstply-do-not-run]` already says not to). The guardrail stays and is what will surface that — `bounded_text` returns the dropped-character count and `llm._text_content` logs it as `extract.text_truncated`, so truncation is counted, never silent. **Do not remove it to tidy up; it is the trigger.** One caveat on the figures: two of the four largest texts (558.164 and 155.288 chars) have no `document` row — never gated, so they cost nothing today but would on a re-extraction. **Also settled here: the IFU exemption Denis was asked to choose already existed** (2026-08-18, one day after this entry was written), so the escalation list quoted below has been stale since; a confident IFU escalates for `['manufacturer', 'ref_list', 'basic_udi_di']` only, and those an IFU genuinely answers — refusing them would lose coverage, not save waste. Was: **CORRECTION 2026-08-27, and it invalidates the half Denis was asked to choose.** He ruled "exempt IFUs first, then size the rest from data" — but **the IFU exemption shipped 2026-08-18, one day AFTER this entry was written**, so the escalation list quoted below (`['validity_from', 'validity_to', 'cert_number', 'manufacturer', 'ref_list', 'basic_udi_di']`) has been stale for nine days and the ruling described work already done. `tiers.py:127-129` drops `_ESCALATE_CERTIFICATE` for a confidently-typed IFU via `_NO_CERTIFICATE_TYPES`. Measured 2026-08-27 rather than read: a confident IFU now escalates for **`['manufacturer', 'ref_list', 'basic_udi_di']`** and nothing else; the same blanks on a DoC still escalate for all six. **And the remainder is not exemptible on the same argument.** "An IFU carries no certificate and no expiry by nature" is true and is spent; what is left is item identity, and an IFU *does* name its product REFs — that is real linking evidence, so refusing to chase it would lose coverage rather than save waste. **So the whole of the remaining work is the second half**: the flat 360.000-char budget and the head-only window. Two distinct problems inside it, worth separating — cost (a per-type budget: a 3-page DoC needs no cap at all) and **correctness** (head-only means a `validity_to` on the last page of a certificate annex is unreachable, which is a wrong answer, not an expensive one, and it no longer has anything to do with IFUs since those stopped chasing dates). Measure what the remaining escalations actually cost, and size the per-type budget from that measurement rather than from a guess — which is the correction this entry already carries once, having predicted ~$0.25 for two IFUs that billed **$0.4155**, because 360.052 characters of Slovene tokenised to **147.289 tokens (2,44 chars/token)**, not the ~90k an English 4-chars/token assumption gives. Do NOT skip to lowering the flat number: 360k is sized so a genuine 40-page multi-product DoC still arrives whole, and cutting it to suit IFUs would silently truncate the documents the registry depends on. The head+tail window stays part of the second half, not the first — `validity_to` on the last page of a certificate annex is unreachable today. Guardrail already in place, do not remove: `bounded_text` returns the dropped-character count and `llm._text_content` logs it as `extract.text_truncated`, so truncation is counted rather than silent. Was: **IMPORTANT — Denis, 2026-08-17: "log it for now, but mark important".** `LLM_TEXT_BUDGET_CHARS` (`app/extract/pdf.py:81`) is one flat 360.000-character number for every document type, and the window it keeps is "the front of the file" for every one of them. That was the right shape for the 400 it was built to stop, and it is the wrong shape for what it costs. Measured the same day on the two STRAUMANN IFUs that forced it: 143 pages / 818.240 chars and 109 pages / 558.164 chars, both previously dead-lettered on `prompt is too long: 341460 tokens > 200000 maximum`, both now clipped to 360.052 chars ≈ 90k tokens. T1 is the only tier that sends text (`llm.py:196`; T2 is vision and already bounded by `MAX_VISION_PAGES`). **Then billed for real on 2026-08-17, which corrected the estimate upward by two thirds:** the two IFUs cost **$0.4155 together** — T1 $0.1428 and $0.1509, T2 $0.0619 and $0.0598 — against the ~$0.25 predicted. The error was the chars/token assumption: 360.052 characters of Slovene tokenized to **147.289 tokens (2,44 chars/token)**, not the ~90k an English ~4 chars/token estimate gives. So one capped IFU costs **~$0.15 on T1 alone, three times the ~$0.05 the whole rest of a document costs**, and the same measurement shows the budget sitting at 74% of the 200k ceiling rather than the 45% originally claimed. The cap made the IFUs safe, not cheap, and less safe than it looked.
      Two things make that spend avoidable rather than merely large. First, the fields the escalation is actually chasing are not spread through the file: after the IFU scope rule shipped, `needs_escalation` on both files returns `['validity_from', 'validity_to', 'cert_number', 'manufacturer', 'ref_list', 'basic_udi_di']`, and every one of those lives on the front matter or the back matter of an IFU, never in the 130 pages of clinical procedure between them. A 90k-token window over the front is paying to read the middle. Second, T0 typed both documents correctly from **1.290 characters of page 1** — the budget is a hundredfold what classification needed.
      The shape to build is a per-type budget plus a per-type window: a `type`-keyed budget map (an IFU is not a DoC and a 3-page DoC needs no cap at all), and head+tail selection rather than head-only, so `validity_to` on the last page of a certificate annex is reachable. Both need the type, which T0 has before any LLM call, so the plumbing exists. Do NOT do this by lowering the flat number — 360k is sized so a genuine 40-page multi-product DoC still arrives whole, and cutting it to suit IFUs would silently truncate the documents the registry actually depends on. Also worth settling in the same pass: whether an IFU should reach T1 at all, given it carries no certificate and no expiry by construction.
      Guardrail already in place, do not remove: `bounded_text` returns the dropped-character count and `llm._text_content` logs it as `extract.text_truncated`, so any truncation is counted rather than silent. Related: `[extract-economics]` (prices as constants) and `[gate-thresholds]`.

- [x] 2026-08-25 [openapi-reachable-with-any-api-key] `S ~1h · found while building the access policy, VERIFY the fix syntax` **`/docs` and `/openapi.json` answer 200 to a caller presenting ANY `X-API-Key` value, valid or not.** Two facts compose into it: (a) Caddy now skips Basic for any request carrying that header (spec §5, the price of "Basic OR a key"), and (b) FastAPI registers its docs/openapi routes as plain Starlette routes via `FastAPI.setup()`, which app-level `dependencies=[...]` do NOT reach — measured, not reasoned: a TestClient with `require_authenticated_user=True` returns `anon=200 service=200 bc=200` for `/openapi.json`, `/docs` and `/static/*`. So `web/access.py`'s default-deny table says staff-only for those paths and does not actually govern them. What leaks is the route/schema listing, not data and not credentials — but it contradicts the stated policy. Fix shapes: scope the Caddy header exclusion to the paths that need it (`not { path /api/* header X-API-Key * }` — group-negation syntax UNVERIFIED, check it compiles), or set `docs_url`/`openapi_url` to a staff-only path the app router actually owns. `/static/*` is the same mechanism and is deliberate — the BC item card needs its stylesheet. **CLOSED 2026-08-25.** `docs_url`/`openapi_url`/`redoc_url` are now None and both paths re-registered through the router, so the app-level guard reaches them; verified through a real Caddy on the dev stack: bogus `X-API-Key` -> 403, was 200. Fixing it surfaced a worse one in the same place -- Caddy's `header_up X-Forwarded-User {http.auth.user.id}` fired on requests that SKIPPED basic_auth and sent the literal unresolved placeholder, which is non-empty, so ANY `X-API-Key` value bought full staff access. Caddyfile now splits into two `handle` blocks (real username when authenticated, header stripped otherwise) and `access.trusted_user()` refuses brace-bearing values as a second layer.

Deliberately OUTSIDE `imports/` -- dropping it there makes `backfill.scan` ingest all 980, which at the measured GC rate of $0.0420/doc is ~$41 of extraction for documents Art. 19 does not require a distributor to hold. **Two corrections to what was said before the files were seen:** (1) secondary sources say a PPWR DoC is drawn up per packaging TYPE; GC issues one per ARTICLE, exactly as their letter claimed -- the letter beat the explainers. (2) GC's letter says the portal is searchable "by article number and product description"; the exposed filter matches product NAME only (`Caviton`, `Fuji Plus` -> 1 asset each; `269071`, `000219`, `10000084` -> zero). **The open measurement:** how much of the catalogue these can REF-match is NOT settled. GC prints dual numbering and is mid-migration -- their MDR DoC table carries `Basic UDI-DI | Article Code | New Article code | Description` (e.g. `++J022MD0105JZ | 000286 | 10000101 | Caviton Jar White 30g`), and the PPWR files key on the 8-digit New Article code. 91 of the 980 are also legacy Art codes (same product, Material == Art code on that row; ZERO cross-product mis-links, checked). `cfg.validate.min_unscoped_ref_len`'s docstring says every real GC corpus match is 6 characters, which suggests the rest miss -- but that records what HAS matched, not that the catalogue lacks 8-digit refs, and if `mfr_ref` has followed GC onto New Article codes then all 980 match. Needs one query against `item_mirror`/`item_group_member` for the length distribution of GC `mfr_ref`; there was no live DB on the machine when this was written (all `$PGDATA_HOST/*` empty). **Answered from the documents 2026-08-26, ahead of the query: there is no numbering margin.** 115 of 124 GC MDR declarations print BOTH spaces -- the 6-digit legacy code and the 8-digit new code, in adjacent columns of the same article table (`000286` and `10000101` for Caviton Jar White 30g). GC is mid-migration and publishes both, so the catalogue is matchable in both. Treat the exposure as all 980, not 91. The `item_mirror` query now confirms rather than decides. Audit: `docs/2026-08-26-ppwr-audit.md` §6.2.
- [x] 2026-08-25 [ppwr-in-scope] `— RULED 2026-08-26 by Denis: NO. Reopen only on a client directive` **PPWR packaging declarations are NOT going into this registry.** Denis, 2026-08-26, after the audit (`docs/2026-08-26-ppwr-audit.md`): they are dangerous and they add nothing, so they stay out unless Dentalia changes the directive. Do not ingest them, do not build a `regulation` value for them, and do not copy `<scratch>/gc-ppwr-2026-08-25/` into `imports/`. What DOES still ship regardless is `[non-md-doc-reaches-production-via-ref]` -- the guard is what enforces this ruling in code rather than in a person's memory. **If the directive changes**, the work is a PRD change first (regulation vocabulary, what `type=DoC` means when the subject is a package, per-article coverage), never a migration on its own. The original question, kept for whoever reopens it: **Does packaging compliance belong in this registry at all?** PPWR is Regulation (EU) 2025/40, applying 12 Aug 2026, repealing packaging directive 94/62/EC. It regulates PACKAGING sustainability -- recyclability, recycled content, substances of concern, labelling, EPR -- and its declaration of conformity is evidence about the box, not the device inside. GC Europe told Dentalia on 2026-08-05 that they now publish one PPWR DoC per article on the same dealer portal as their MDR DoCs; 980 of them are archived at `<scratch>/gc-ppwr-2026-08-25/` (see `[gc-ppwr-archive]`). **The argument for NOT adopting:** three independent secondary sources agree that Art. 19 distributor duties are to verify producer registration (Art. 44), labelling (Art. 12) and manufacturer/importer identification (Arts. 15(5)-(6), 18(3)) -- holding or archiving the DoC is not among them, and the duty to draw it up and retain it (5 y single-use, 10 y reusable) sits with the packaging manufacturer. On that reading GC is being helpful, not discharging an obligation of Dentalia's, and ingesting 980 documents per manufacturer costs ~$41 each at the measured GC rate against a €10-40/month steady-state target. **The argument FOR:** Art. 19 also sets a "due care" standard and bars a distributor from making packaging available it believes non-conforming; keeping the supplier's declaration is defensible evidence of having exercised it. That is a legal call for Dentalia, not an engineering one. **Verification limit on the above:** the primary EUR-Lex text could not be retrieved (five attempts, WebFetch x3 and curl x2, all empty bodies) -- the Art. 19 content is from secondary sources that agree with each other, not from the regulation. Confirm against the actual text before acting on it commercially. **If the answer is yes**, it is a PRD change before any code: the `regulation` vocabulary, what `type=DoC` means when the subject is a package, and per-article coverage. **If no**, nothing to build -- but `[non-md-doc-reaches-production-via-ref]` still ships regardless, because these files reach the pipeline through the SFTP dump without anyone deciding anything.
