- [x] 2026-08-21 [serving-mechanism-and-doc-names] **RULED AND BUILT 2026-08-25 (Denis's ruling, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-25-item-document-access-design.md`).** All three gaps closed. (1) Mechanism: **there is no public, unauthenticated tier.** The webshop backend fetches server-to-server and relays the bytes to its own logged-in clinics under its own origin; BC gets an item-card hyperlink carrying a shared static key. An HMAC per-item capability URL was designed and REJECTED -- a BC hyperlink is a computed field built by string concatenation, so BC cannot compute a signature, and with the shop relaying there is no public surface for a capability URL to protect (spec §0). (2) Display name: derived, not stored -- latest-revision `manufacturer` evidence + type + regulation + effective expiry, in `web/item_docs.py`, shared by both routes so they cannot drift. (3) Credentials: three caller classes in `web/access.py` (`staff` Basic auth, `service` `X-API-Key` from a rotatable `WEB_API_KEYS` list, `bc` `?k=WEB_BC_LINK_KEY`), default-deny route policy, `hmac.compare_digest` throughout, Caddy exclusions so the two non-browser classes reach the app that owns their credentials. Slice 2 (manufacturer catalogue + order batch) and rate limiting are logged separately below. Was: `M · needs Denis` The read API is deliberately minimal (S1.6; ruling "expose a read API from day one, wire nothing yet"): `GET /api/items/{item_ref}/documents` is `SELECT * FROM item_document_production` (`web/app.py:2910`) — type, regulation, dates, `archive_url`, nothing else. Three gaps stand between that and the stated end-use (webshop/BC/support asks for an item, gets JSON of links with names and types): (1) the consumer mechanism is undecided — direct exposure vs links relayed through the webshop or BC (Denis 2026-08-21: "not sure yet... this mechanism is not yet fixed"); (2) `document` has no display-name field, so the response cannot carry a human-readable name — derive one (manufacturer + type + validity year) or add a stored title; (3) auth is the Caddy basic-auth ingress only, no per-consumer credential story. A fourth defect is already tracked as [task4-cert-view] (the view serves raw `validity_to`, so the API returns a null expiry for a DoC whose date sits on its cited certificate) — not re-logged here. (1) blocks (2) and (3); nothing to build until the mechanism is ruled.

- [x] 2026-08-17 [latest-extract-rev-can-be-the-poorest] `— record only, nothing to build` **FALSE ALARM, resolved the same hour it was raised. Recorded so nobody spends an afternoon on it again.** The observation was real: exactly 2 of 314 content hashes have a latest `extract_rev` holding fewer fields than an earlier rev, both the Slovene GC safety data sheets (`gc-tissue-conditioner-powder-sds-slo.pdf`, `...-liquid-sds-slo.pdf`), whose rev 1 (`T0+T1+T2`, 08-12) and rev 2 (08-13) carry values while **rev 3 (`T0` only, 08-14 11:36) is `{}`** — and GATE reads `max(extract_rev)`, so `{}` is what it sees.
      The inference was wrong. `{}` is not a degraded replay shadowing good data; it is the 2026-08-13 change ("fold whitespace before matching a document-class marker") working as intended. pymupdf renders those pages double-spaced, so `Varnostni  list` never matched the `varnostni list` marker; once it did, both documents classified as `msds` and correctly short-circuited with no LLM call (`tiers.py:235`, behaviour dating to a 2026-07-06 commit). That commit measured the blast radius at the time: "exactly 2 documents change class, both the SDS this was reported for" — the same two.
      So the **later, emptier rev is the correct one**, and revs 1-2 were the error being corrected: full T1+T2 spend on a safety data sheet. Both documents are legitimately absent from the registry and should stay absent; do not re-enqueue them and do not "fix" the rev selection to prefer the deeper ladder, which would resurrect exactly the wrong extraction. The theoretical risk (a shallow replay shadowing a deeper one) has no instance in the corpus that is not this intended case.
- [x] 2026-08-17 [expiry-already-lapsed] **CLOSED 2026-08-18.** Split into expired-in-the-last-180-days (leads, it is the emergency), expiring-in-the-next-180, and long-expired (collapsed, kept). Anything beyond the window is counted and named. Was: `XS ~30m · UI` `/expiry` is framed as a horizon board ("what is about to lapse", 30/60/90/180 days) but its filter is `expires <= current_date + days`, so everything already expired is in scope too — and on the live registry that is ALL of it: three rows, lapsed 813, 813 and 105 days ago, one of them covering 1.046 catalogue items. The collapse work (2026-08-17) made this legible for the first time by putting days-left in a column; it did not address it. An already-lapsed certificate is a different and worse problem than one lapsing next month, and the board should say so — a separate section or a status column, not a negative number in a field labelled "days left". Do not simply filter them out: 1.046 items sitting on a certificate that expired in May is the single most urgent thing the registry currently knows.
- [x] 2026-08-17 [gate-bind-zero-links] **CLOSED 2026-08-19.** Raises, as this entry prescribed. **One correction to what it says below:** it claims the review UI "no longer offers a name that would" -- the picker does not, but `POST /staging/{doc_id}/apply` (`web/app.py:2426`) validates only that the manufacturer string is non-empty, so the dropdown is the sole guard and it is client-side. A live route reached this, not only a replayed or hand-built payload. The handler runs inside the runner's transaction, so the raise takes the promote and both audit rows with it -- asserted, because a raise that left the document on production would be the worse bug. The existing `test_bind_manufacturer_on_an_unknown_name_links_nothing` was rewritten rather than deleted: its point, that an unresolvable name must not widen to the rest of the alias table, is unchanged. Was: `S ~45m · handler + test` `_derive_mfr_scope_links` (`gate.py:851`) returns silently when its name resolves to no BC codes, so a `bind-manufacturer` apply promotes the document to `production`, links zero items, and writes `bind-manufacturer` + `production-write` audit entries that read like a success. C16's machine path cannot hit it (it counts items before choosing `production` over `filed`, `gate.py:586-592`) and the review UI no longer offers a name that would (2026-08-17: the picker lists only entities holding an MD item, and resolves the payload's raw name through `_mfr_bind_target` before offering one click). What remains open is the handler itself: a hand-built `gate.apply` payload, a replayed old job, or a future producer can still walk into it. Fix is to raise rather than return — a job that fails is visible, a bind that links nothing is not. Deliberately left web-only on 2026-08-17 (Denis's call) because it crosses into the one module that writes the registry and wants its own test against real Postgres.

Original entry: **Confirmed Critical, final review.** A DoC can inherit its renewal date from a certificate a human REJECTED. `_resolve_cited_certificate` (`validate.py:219`) correctly restricts to `production`/`superseded`, but every read surface that follows `cert_doc_id` joins it unfiltered: `report.py:36` (weekly report + scheduler expiry scan), `web/app.py:501` (`/items` next_expiry), `:533` (`/items/{ref}`), `:638` (`/expiry`). Reachability is the normal path, not a corner case: an EC cert with no REF list gets `no-item-identifier` and lands `staged` by default. Fix is read-side in all four places, `AND c.status IN ('production','superseded')`, matching the forward path. Opus verification rejected the handler-side alternative: gating back-resolution on status destroys fetch-order independence, because the certificate shape that most needs back-resolution is exactly the one that lands staged.
      **PARTIALLY CLOSED 2026-08-14.** `report.py:36` is fixed -- the weekly report and the scheduler's expiry scan no longer inherit a rejected certificate's date. The filter goes on the JOIN, not the WHERE: on a LEFT join, WHERE makes it inner and drops every DoC that carries its own expiry and cites nothing. Mutation-checked both ways, since tightening to production-only is its own bug -- a superseded certificate was real and its dates still describe the period it covered. **STILL OPEN: the three `web/app.py` reads** (`:501` `/items` next_expiry, `:533` `/items/{ref}`, `:638` `/expiry`), left because a parallel session held the file uncommitted. Display-only, but they now disagree with the report. Also confirmed while fixing this: VALIDATE's forward path needed NO change -- it already excluded rejected certificates -- and a test now pins that, because nothing did.

Was: `S ~30m` Two unhandled 500s on user input. `/items?page=` has no upper bound (`web/app.py:476`): psycopg sends an oversized int as `numeric` and `OFFSET` coerces to `bigint`, so a value past ~9.2e16 raises. `/archive/{path}` with a percent-encoded null byte (`%00`) raises `ValueError: embedded null byte` (`web/app.py:434`). Neither is a traversal bypass — absolute paths, encoded `..` and symlinks were all verified rejected — just uncaught input classes. `days` already got this treatment (2026-08-10); apply the same to `page` and catch the null byte.
- [x] 2026-08-10 [results-anomaly-kinds] **CLOSED 2026-08-19.** Done as the re-scope described: two implements and two deletes. `reclassified_non_md` and `cert_reference_unresolved` now raise beside their existing signal; `code_shape_unexpected` and `blocked_item` are gone, with the reasoning kept in the frozenset's comment and `docs/vocabulary.md` so both come back the day there is a rule to implement. No migration, as predicted. One thing the re-scope did not foresee: VALIDATE's anomaly could not go beside the flag, because the normal path's `Result` is not constructed until further down -- it is raised there instead, off `flags`. Was: **RE-SCOPED 2026-08-19: it is FOUR kinds, not three, and the answer is two implements and two deletes -- not one either/or.** The fourth is `cert_reference_unresolved`, filed separately as `[task4-cert-anomaly]`; the two entries are the same defect seen twice and should be worked together. **No migration is needed for any of it**: `migrations/019_job_result.sql` declares `data_anomaly.kind` as plain `text NOT NULL` with no CHECK and no enum -- the only enforcement is the `ValueError` in `app/results.py` `Result.anomaly()`, so deleting a kind is an edit to the frozenset, its comment, and the prose list in `docs/vocabulary.md`, plus whatever `tests/test_vocabulary_doc.py` cross-checks. Per kind:
  - `reclassified_non_md` -- **implement.** `app/handlers/ingest.py` counts it (`r.count`) in the non-MD branch where every sibling branch in the same handler also calls `r.anomaly(...)` beside its count (`md_class_blank`, `mfr_ref_missing`, `mfr_ref_prose`, `manufacturer_code_blank` all do). One line, same shape as its neighbours. Until then `/data-quality` shows nothing for it however many items BC declassifies.
  - `cert_reference_unresolved` -- **implement**, tracked at `[task4-cert-anomaly]`. VALIDATE appends the FLAG `cert-unresolved` and nothing else. Flags and anomalies are two deliberately separate vocabularies with different consumers (`docs/vocabulary.md` says so): the flag reaches `gate.py`'s `INFORMATIONAL_FLAGS` and shapes the disposition, the anomaly reaches the `data_anomaly` ledger and `/data-quality`. Both are wanted, from the one detection. Renaming either vocabulary into the other is the wrong fix -- they are structurally different (a flag is an inline string on one candidate, an anomaly is a keyed cross-run row with `seen_count`/`first_seen`/`last_seen`).
  - `code_shape_unexpected` -- **delete.** Not in the contract PRD, the ground truth, the schema sketch or the job-type handbook (zero grep hits in all four). It exists as an inline comment ("manufacturer code breaks the 3/5-digit pattern") and one motivating anecdote in a session plan. No stage was ever named as its producer and no shape-validation logic exists anywhere in `app/`. There is nothing to implement it against.
  - `blocked_item` -- **delete, and re-add if the client rules.** Traces only to `docs/2026-08-11-open-questions-for-dentalia.md` §F question 25 (`Blokirano = 1`, 678 rows, 43 of them classified as devices, "Currently out of scope"), still unanswered in the round-2 replies. Implementing a producer now would mean inventing the MDR retention policy for a blocked previously-sold item, which is the client's call, not ours.
  The principle the original entry got right and is worth keeping: a closed vocabulary with unreachable members reads as coverage that does not exist. Was: Three `ANOMALY_KINDS` beyond `[task4-cert-anomaly]`'s have no producer anywhere in `app/`: `code_shape_unexpected`, `reclassified_non_md`, `blocked_item`. `reclassified_non_md` is *counted* by INGEST but never emitted as an anomaly, so `/data-quality` shows nothing for it however many items BC declassifies. Either wire the producers or trim the vocabulary: a closed vocabulary with unreachable members reads as coverage that does not exist.

The shipped `_import_preview.html` only carries explanatory copy saying the apply re-diffs; it renders one set of counts, whichever job you are looking at. The filename fix (2026-08-26) already proved the additive-payload route works, so this is the same shape of change.  **Done 2026-08-26**: `preview_job_id` rides the apply payload (additive at enqueue, invariant 9 intact; INGEST ignores it), `import_preview` reads the referenced preview's result, and `IMPORT_COUNT_ROWS` is walked twice into a Previewed/Applied table that highlights and names every counter that moved. Degrades to one column when the preview job is pruned or the payload predates the field.

The review UI strips the `{hash12}__` archive prefix back off (`_download_name`, web/app.py) but cannot restore what sanitization removed. - [x] 2026-08-12 [stale-image-trap] **CLOSED 2026-08-12.** `app/version.py`'s `source_fingerprint()` hashes `app/**/*.py` + `migrations/*.sql` at runtime, path-independently, so `python -m app.cli version` answers identically on host and in-container and the worker prints it in its first log line (`runner.py:110`). Deliberately not a build-time `ARG GIT_SHA` — a build arg is one more thing to forget, which reproduces the mismatch. Was: **The compose stack will silently run months-old code against a current database, and nothing warns.** `docker compose up -d worker` starts the existing `dentalia-app:latest` without rebuilding, so on 2026-08-12 the worker ran an image built `2026-08-11T08:25:56Z` — four hours older than the 2026-08-11 commit, which introduced the `ref-catalogue` linking path. It processed all 61 `validate.doc` jobs "successfully", wrote 57 documents and 307 evidence rows, dead-lettered nothing, and produced **zero** links; the only symptom was the flag set (`ref-unscoped 10` from the old code, where current code emits `ref-catalogue`/`no-ref-overlap`). Confirmed by `docker exec dentalia-worker-1 grep -c ref-catalogue /app/app/handlers/validate.py` returning 0 against 9 on the host. A green run against a stale image is indistinguishable from a correct one without diffing behaviour — worth either a build-stamp the worker logs at startup (git SHA baked in at build, compared against `schema_migrations` or just printed), or a documented "always `docker compose build` before `up`" step in the runbook. Recovery was free here because all three GATE writes are idempotent (`ON CONFLICT` on `document`/`item_document`, `NOT EXISTS` on `evidence`), so re-emitting the jobs replayed cleanly with evidence staying at 307 rows.
- [x] 2026-08-11 [queue-pending-paid] 87 `extract.doc` + 61 `validate.doc` jobs pending in the dev queue. The extract jobs are PAID work that runs the moment a worker starts — decide whether to drain, clear, or leave them before starting any worker. **CLOSED 2026-08-11:** measured the real exposure from the cost ledger first — $2,92 spent on 60 documents ($0,0487 average; T1 $0,008, T2 $0,047), so the 87 were worth roughly **$4**, not the open-ended risk the entry implied. Denis chose to clear them: all 87 jobs and their 87 `fetch_log` rows deleted (zero overlap with the 61 extracted, zero `doc_id` references, all `source=backfill`), regenerability then **verified** by a rolled-back re-scan. Re-enqueue with `enqueue backfill.scan gc-rescan --payload '{"drive_folder": ".../imports/dentalia-sftp/GC"}'`. Row-level backup was session-scoped scratchpad only, so the re-scan is the real recovery path. See `[backfill-not-regenerable]` and `[worker-type-filter]`, both found while closing this.
- [x] 2026-08-12 [backfill-bytes-not-archived] **Closed same day by the parallel session.** Was: `backfill.scan` recorded `archive_url = str(path.resolve())` into `imports/` and never archived the bytes, so `/archive` held 0 files while 1,303 corpus PDFs sat under `/imports` — host paths that existed nowhere inside the container. Now: the scan puts bytes through the StorageAdapter and stores what the store returns, so `archive_url` is `/archive/{brand}/{type}/{hash12}__{name}.pdf`, uniform across worker and web, and the worker mounts `/imports:ro` so the scan runs in-container (running it on the host is what recorded the host paths). `_resolve_archive_path` (web/app.py) now resolves every document on its first branch; `WEB_PATH_REWRITES` and the hash-verified tail search stop being load-bearing and stay only as a safety net for pre-existing rows (Denis's call, 2026-08-12 — keep rather than rip out).

Rather than ratify an invariant-1 exception, the module is removed: it existed for 57 rows that the registry wipe erased, and the producer bug that created them is fixed at source (backfill archives through the StorageAdapter), so no foreign handle can be written again. Invariant 1 stays absolute: only `gate.candidate` / `gate.apply` write the registry. Recoverable from git if a future ingestion path ever reintroduces non-archive handles.

- [x] 2026-08-13 [gate-never-retracts-a-link] **CLOSED same day (migration 024).** Staged links the current rev omits are now retracted to the new `retracted` status (distinct from `rejected`, which records a human); production links are never auto-retracted, which is what makes it safe without a provenance column, since approving is what promotes a link. `_is_current_rev` also stops a superseded candidate writing at all. Applied: 21 retracted, 113 live, 113 supported by the current rev, 21 audit events, $0. Was: **A link outlives the extraction that justified it.** GATE writes `item_document` with an idempotent upsert and never removes a link the CURRENT `extract_rev` no longer supports, so coverage can only ever grow and a corrected extraction cannot take back a wrong link. Proven on document 235: rev 1 (a REF list truncated to 40 codes) resolved to one manufacturer and wrote 19 links; rev 2 (the repaired 1.121-code list) spans GC EUROPE N.V. and HENRY SCHEIN, correctly flags `multi-manufacturer-ref` and carries **0** links — and job 30203 (rev 2) ran AFTER 30196 (rev 1) with the 19 links still standing. Measured 2026-08-13 across the registry: 134 rows in `item_document`, **113** supported by the current rev, **21 stale**. In a compliance registry this is the wrong direction of error: it claims coverage the evidence no longer supports. The fix belongs in GATE (invariant 1 — nothing else may write links) and there is precedent for GATE changing link status, since `gate.apply` `reject` already cascades links to `rejected`. Decide the semantics first: retract to a `superseded`/`rejected` link status rather than deleting (invariant 4 is append-only), and only for links whose doc_id+item_ref the newest rev's candidate omits. Note the interaction with `[doc-covers-one-group-only]`: a document whose enumeration GROWS can newly span two manufacturers and lose its links entirely, which is correct but only visible if retraction works.

Measured 2026-08-13 AFTER the ref_list repair: 3.118 codes stored reach **323** catalogue items, **96** links were written, so **228 items are missed** across 23 documents spanning more than one group. The pair (manufacturer, article number) holds for all 20, so there is no trust reason to drop 19. Denis's ruling 2026-08-13: "all items must get the document attached if a document covers multiple items" — a declaration listing 20 articles covers 20 articles, and `item_group` is OUR clustering for discovery, not the manufacturer's statement of coverage. Fix: link every member matching the REF list across all groups under the pinned manufacturer, keeping `_manufacturers_holding_refs`' exactly-one-holder guard untouched (that is what preserves invariant 3) and keeping the lowest group as the document's primary for provenance. `multi-group-match` then loses its reason to cap, since nothing is arbitrary any more — decide that when it becomes live, as everything caps at `ref-catalogue` regardless until the manufacturer resolves. - [x] 2026-09-02 [fetch-stores-html-as-a-document] **DONE 2026-09-02** — magic-byte gate `_is_document` in `app/handlers/fetch.py` (2026-09-02) refuses a non-document body before `store.put`, outcome `not-a-document`; `promote_doc_class` in `app/extract/t0_templates.py` (2026-09-02) stops T0 typing an untyped body as `compliance-doc` on a stray keyword. Verified live: the same 5 groups re-run produced 0 documents (was 4), and the Duerr button produced 11 real documents with 10 web pages refused. Original report below.
  `S ~2h · blocks automatic discovery` **FETCH has no content-type guard, so an HTML page fetched from a `.pdf` URL is archived, extracted, typed and staged as a compliance document.** `app/handlers/fetch.py:180` passes `resp.content_type` to `archiving.archive_path` for the folder/extension only; nothing anywhere refuses a non-document body. PyMuPDF then paginates HTML happily (it supports HTML as an input format), so EXTRACT produces plausible output on website navigation text and the whole chain reports success. **Measured on the first production run of the search rung, 2026-09-02** (5 scoped groups, jobs 35718-35722): 4 of 4 documents produced were HTML — docs 853/854/855/856, magic bytes `<!doc`, `<!DOC`, `<!DOC`, `<!DO`. Worst case is 853: `http://www.bredent.com/en/bredent/download/Englisch_Declaration_of_Conformity_54001167.pdf` returns **HTTP 200 `Content-Type: text/html`, 301.268 bytes** (verified with curl the same day), and it was archived as `.../BREDENT/doc/...Declaration_of_Conformity_54001167.pdf`, rendered to 22 pages of bredent's site menu, and typed `IFU` at T0 confidence 1.00 off the words "Instructions for use" somewhere in the navigation. The other three are manualslib.com manual pages and solventum.com's `/en-us/home/resources/` landing page. **The safety net held** — all four are `status='staged'` with one `fetch-context` link capped at staged, so nothing reached production; the cost is a polluted review queue and a coverage number that counts web pages. Fix: refuse (or route to a distinct outcome) a body whose sniffed magic bytes are not a document, and record the reason on the job rather than dropping it silently. **This should land before `[discover-coverage-cron]`**: automatic discovery at catalogue scale would mass-produce these.
- [x] 2026-09-02 [gate-covers-only-fetch] **DONE 2026-09-02** — lifted to `archiving.is_document` and called by FETCH, `upload.ingest`, and the upload form (`web/app.py`), which checks the bytes while the person is still there and says what to do instead. **Two corrections to the report below:** `email_poll` was NOT unguarded — `_is_pdf` (`app/handlers/email_poll.py:75`) already searched the first 1024 bytes for `%PDF` and refused container signatures, deliberately wider than the archive gate because a mail attachment may carry a preamble. And the form was not unchecked — it checked the filename and the browser's content-type, which is exactly what the bredent file defeats. So the gap was one writer plus a byte check on the form, not four writers. `backfill.scan` is deliberately left, see `[gate-covers-only-backfill]`. 9 tests added. Original report below.
  `S ~2h` The document gate added 2026-09-02 (`_is_document`, `app/handlers/fetch.py`) guards **one** of five archive writers. `store.put` is also called by `app/handlers/backfill.py` (twice), `app/handlers/upload.py` and `app/handlers/email_poll.py`, none of which check the body. **The registry is still protected** — all four emit `extract.doc`, so T0's header-window rule and the doc-class call refuse a non-document before any `document` row exists — but the *archive* can still receive one. The realistic path is the web upload: a person picks the wrong file and an HTML page or a screenshot lands under `/archive/<MFR>/`, where nothing ever reads it and the magic-byte scan that verified the archive clean on 2026-09-02 would start reporting it. Lift `_is_document` into `app/handlers/archiving.py` beside `archive_path` and call it from all five; for `upload.ingest` the refusal wants a message on the form, not a job result, since a human is standing there.

- [x] 2026-08-27 [migration-041-merged-still-duplicated] `DONE 2026-08-27: no renumber, guard shipped` **Denis delegated the call ("renumber if we must"); the answer is we must not.** Three findings, measured the same day: (a) renumbering would break every database that already migrated -- `run_migrations` tracks by `path.name`, so a renamed file re-runs, and `041_eudamed_job_types` (2 bare `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE`), `043` (6 bare `CREATE VIEW`) and `046` (4 bare `CREATE VIEW`) carry no `IF NOT EXISTS` / `OR REPLACE`, so the re-run errors; the dev database has all 48 migrations applied. (b) The premise of the original merge-time ruling is gone -- it turned on `playbooks-into-db` holding 046/047, and that branch has since moved to **049/050/051**, so master's 041-046 collides with nothing. (c) The pair is functionally inert: both 041s are a single `ALTER TYPE job_type ADD VALUE` (`vendor.import` vs `eudamed.certregister`/`eudamed.sweep`), independent and order-irrelevant, so the "fresh database applies them in the other order" hazard has no effect. **DONE 2026-08-27 -- and built against a different invariant than this entry proposed.** The duplicate-prefix guard was the wrong check twice over: 18 of 20 branches carry the unrenameable `014_evidence_rev` / `014_upload` pair so it could never refuse, and it would have missed the `032_stated_class` + `033_item_class_check` after `034_item_document_production_expiry` episode entirely, which has no collision anywhere in it. What shipped is `db._out_of_order`: pending files that sort below the highest already-applied one, i.e. the case where a fresh database applies them in a different relative order than this one did. Warn by default (a lower-numbered branch is routine and gets fixed at merge time, which is when `migrate` still has to run), `strict=True` raises `OutOfOrderMigration` before applying anything and `tests/conftest.py` uses it so CI fails loudly. The dev database stays quiet -- nothing pending, nothing to judge. Runbook documents the warning and carries the applied-vs-disk query. Original finding: The eudamed merge landed on master (2026-08-27) and the duplicate 041 came with it: master now holds both `041_vendor_import.sql` and `041_eudamed_job_types.sql`. **The reason the 2026-08-27 ruling said "renumber at merge time" has since evaporated** — that ruling turned on `playbooks-into-db` holding 046/047, and that branch has since renumbered itself to **049/050/051**, so master's 041-046 no longer collides with anything. Renumbering now is strictly worse than it was on the branch: `run_migrations` tracks by filename, so renaming `041_eudamed_job_types` -> `047_` makes every dev database that already applied it apply it again under the new name, across all six eudamed files. Recommendation: leave the pair, and instead take the guard the original entry proposed — make `_migration_files` refuse a duplicate numeric prefix outright, which turns the next collision from silent into loud and costs four lines. Supersedes the merge-time half of `[migration-041-collision]` (line ~50) and closes the open question in `[migration-041-collides]`; both are left in place as the record.

Original finding: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-25-eudamed-enhancement.md` gives `docker compose run --rm --no-deps test python -m pytest ...` as the run command in **22** places. ### Triage, 2026-08-27 — the 30-day sweep

_Parts (b) and (c) of `[followups-triage-overdue]`, done at Denis's word. Part
(a) — closing what shipped — was done the same day by the drift check: 41
entries moved into `## Done`, 13 open ones lifted back out of it._

**37 of the 171 open entries are older than 30 days (before 2026-07-28), and
NOT ONE of them is dead.** That is the finding, and it was measured rather than
assumed: every claim cheap enough to check was checked against the tree, and
all still hold — `PRICES` is still a constant dict with no `[pricing]` config
section; both T1 rankers still raise `NotImplementedError` (`resolve.py:269`,
`discover.py:172`); `_apply_manufacturer_bindings` still returns 0 by design;
`app/urls.py` still declines `utm_*` stripping on purpose; `LJ_ODATA_PROFILE`
still has no `name_fallback`; `job.result` is still absent from `/jobs/{id}`
(it IS rendered on `/ingest` and `/import`, so that entry is narrower than it
reads); the Starlette deprecation still logs on every test run.

**So the backlog is not rot. It is unscheduled work behind real gates**, and
the useful axis is not age but what each one waits on:

| Gate | Old entries | What it means |
|---|---|---|
| **Waits on the paid sweep** | `[fetch-js-challenge]`, `[fetch-domain-flag-persist]`, `[url-normalize-refine]`, `[extract-economics]` (ledger half), `[extract-ladder-scope]` | Each says "only if the sweep shows it" in its own text. The sweep has not run. Working these now guesses at data we get for free later — leave them |
| **Waits on Denis** | `[extract-verify]`, `[extract-tests]` (review half), `[extract-findings]`, `[discover-thresholds]`, `[discover-tier-a]`, `[udi-group-key]` | Already indexed in *Blocked on you*. No agent can start these |
| **Inert by design** | `[resolve-c4]`, `[web-ui]` | Not defects. Listed so nobody re-discovers them. `[web-ui]` is a third-party deprecation warning with no failure behind it |
| **Available now, no gate** | `[extract-t0]` ×4, `[extract-economics]` (config half), `[validate-report]`, `[validate-flags]`, `[validate-misc]`, `[s1.1-ingest]` ×5, `[ingress-map]`, `[discover-handbook-ratify]`, `[corpus-verify]` ×2, `[extract-scope]` | Small, self-contained. **This is the real queue: ~18 entries, not 171** |
| **Phase 2, deliberately** | `[sftp-source]` (audit + adapter) | The corpus is already on disk from a manual dump; the adapter is for when it must refresh itself |

**Two clusters are one piece of work each and should close together.**
`[extract-scope]` (T0 types a cosmetics/REACH/LVD declaration as a compliance
doc), `[cosmetics-doc-at-production]` (one reached production) and
`[non-md-doc-reaches-production-via-ref]` (GATE's REF branch has no device
check) are the same defect seen from three stages — the guard in the third
closes all three, and it is what enforces the PPWR no-go in code rather than in
someone's memory. Separately `[expiry-flag-name]`,
`[expiry-flag-should-be-about-evidence]` and `[expiry-flag-history]` are one
flag's history: the third is explicitly record-only and superseded, the first
is a rename waiting on the second.

**What this pass did NOT do:** re-verify the 134 entries newer than 30 days.
They are inside the window this triage was defined to leave alone, and saying
so is the point — a triage that quietly widens its own scope is how the last
one never happened.

- [x] 2026-09-04 [gate-upsert-resurrects-filed-and-rejected] `M ~3h + a ruling` **`gate.py::_upsert_document`'s ON CONFLICT protects only `production` and `superseded`.** `status = CASE WHEN document.status IN ('production','superseded') THEN document.status ELSE EXCLUDED.status END` — so ANY re-delivery of a candidate for a `filed` or `rejected` document overwrites its status, with **no audit entry**. Measured live 2026-09-04 by `repair-label-dates --apply` (67 re-emitted `validate.doc`): 4 documents moved, 3 `filed`→`staged` and **doc 837 `rejected`→`staged`, undoing a human rejection made by `admin` on 2026-08-25**. All four were restored by hand from a pre-run snapshot; the dates and `superseded_by` never moved, only `status`. **CLOSED 2026-09-04**: `_upsert_document` now refuses every backward step (`staged < filed < production`; `rejected`/`superseded` terminal), with a table-driven test over all eight transitions. `filed`->`production` stays allowed, which is why the naive fix was wrong. Left open by it: `[no-doc-level-reopen]`. **This was not specific to that tool** — `repair_ref_list` re-emits the same way, and so does any ordinary re-extraction, so the exposure is the whole re-delivery path. The fix is NOT simply adding both to the protected list: `filed`→`production` is a legitimate promotion when a re-extraction finds items (`status = "production" if n_items else "filed"`), so protecting `filed` outright would block it. The rule wanted is "never move BACKWARD" (staged < filed < production; rejected and superseded terminal), which is a real ordering decision and needs Denis's ruling on whether a machine may ever un-reject. Until then: **snapshot `document.status` before any bulk re-emission and diff after.**
- [x] 2026-09-04 [no-doc-level-reopen] **CLOSED 2026-09-04.** `gate.apply reopen` is the deliberate way out: precondition `status='rejected'`, raises otherwise, audited under the name that asked, and NO link cascade — `reject` cascades links so none is left staged under a rejected document, and re-staging them would recreate exactly that state, so each returns through `reopen-link`. UI is a card on the document page, visible only when the document is rejected. Both guide languages updated: the Documents page said "Can I break anything here? No", which this card made untrue. Original entry: **A rejected DOCUMENT has no way back.** `gate.apply` has `reopen-link` (`LINK_DECISIONS`, `gate.py:1318`) for a rejected LINK and no equivalent for the document itself; the only doc-level write to `rejected` is `gate.py:1442` and nothing reverses it. This did not matter while any re-delivery silently un-rejected documents, which is exactly the bug `[gate-upsert-resurrects-filed-and-rejected]` closed on 2026-09-04 -- so closing it turned an accidental escape into no escape at all. **I told Denis a UI path existed when I asked for the ruling; it does not, and the ruling was given on that wrong premise.** The fix follows the shape already there: a `reopen` decision in `gate.apply`, precondition `status='rejected'`, audited like every other human verb, and it must NOT cascade links back (a reopened document's links stay rejected until `reopen-link` says otherwise, which is the state the reject cascade exists to keep clean).
